**Changes:** No changes made. All findings (1–8) carried recommendations of none; no excisions were indicated, so the restored chapter text is returned unchanged, including lacunose damage in ¶2 and ¶16 and the preserved redundancy in ¶18.

**Spiritual narrative:** The disorder that burst forth from governing falsities and evils as a gross external principle rose up against the revealed pattern of Divine mediation. It was severed from that higher place and cast down into the lowest degree, because ordered interior states would not admit it into their harmonies. When it entered the lowest level of life it organized itself into a fixed system of perverted perception, established itself in that system, and produced works from it; and when those malformed works again collapsed into the lowest level, the same disorder returned within them and re‑entered the governing falsities.

Within those works it took on their quality and spoke through the chief ruling falsity. That leading falsity urged its allied falsities to contribute their borrowed insight so that a human likeness could be fabricated, modeled on the loftier pattern. They yielded what they had, and from it the imitation of true humanity was produced.

That same gross external influence—which had already perceived the Divine pattern, organized a perverted system, and lodged within it—afterward rose again within the resulting works and passed into the governing powers. By its driving force they produced an inner and outer human appearance after the loftier pattern. Through the efficacy of this disorder, entering by means of those works, it took possession of the rulers; and under its impulse they generated successive states from themselves, each according to its ruling inclination, some retaining a likeness to the true form and others losing it.

So understand this: the lower governing powers shaped the first human state through the activity of disorder entering them by means of works, and thus established a human form according to the loftier pattern.

Then the formative power arranged within that human form capacities for receiving and acting, outwardly and inwardly, distributed into ordered regions. For whatever these capacities and their elemental operations take in from the outermost life, there are inward retentive powers and ordered recesses, so that what is received can be preserved. When inquiry is made for what has been deposited within, each capacity draws out what it received and supplies it to the one who seeks it.

Thus the outward capacity for discernment is furnished with power to notice every kind of presented quality and to keep each kind within its inward retention.

Likewise, that same outward discernment has inward retentive powers capable of receiving every appearance it takes in—whether aligned with good or with evil, whether it stirs love, aversion, or craving. When it chooses, it can enter its inner retentions, search and recollect what was stored, and bring it forward for the inquirer—whether it serves craving, genuine love, or aversion; and so it acts according to the kind of reception.

The capacity that receives instruction also holds what it takes in—whether good or evil—within inner memory, preserving it through long duration. Even after long duration, when inquiry is made for what it once received, it immediately searches its inner records, finds that particular teaching, and produces it from the state in which it was first preserved.

In the same way, the capacity that discerns subtle quality takes in what it perceives and deposits it in inward memory; when questioned it returns to those memories and recalls only what it retained.

Further, the capacity that discerns appropriations and delights gathers such perceptions into its own inward retentions; whenever inquiry is made about a particular delight, it can recall it and supply even its distinctive sign, giving the remembered quality to the one who asks.

Again, the capacity of direct contact with the outermost receives the memory of that contact and stores it inwardly until inquiry is made; then it returns, draws out what it retained, and gives it to whoever asks.

Over all these is the central governing love-and-thought which presides as the chief faculty: whatever the five outward capacities receive and entrust to it, it receives and guards; and whenever their deposit is requested, it brings forth and returns all that was given to it.

For the deliberation of this central faculty is like a ruling authority: with it are subordinate reasonings and directive powers for action; when these ask for what they desire, it supplies the judgment and counsel by which they proceed.

And it is also like an inward steward: whatever is entrusted to it, it receives and arranges distinctly in ordered forms and distinct states, and it can produce each again from the very state in which it was placed.

So too the coordinating power for outward action can bring forth again, from inward retention, whatever discernment has stored and whatever is requested.

Likewise it can bring forth again whatever receptivity to instruction has stored, whenever that remembered teaching is called for.

The remaining outward capacities follow the same order: what each has stored can be produced and supplied whenever it is requested.

Yet protections stand at these entrances, and restraints are fixed there under the authority of those protections.

It is like a guarded system of admission: restraints are set upon the approaches; vigilant powers govern those restraints at the point of entry. What is native to the ruling love and agrees with it is admitted; what is foreign to it is resisted and held outside; and something else may be admitted only with difficulty. Within are many inner containments and many controlling restraints; and when the directing power chooses, it handles the restraints, opens admission, and draws out what had been hidden.

So the whole outward life is like a powerful organized domain, and its admissions and their guards are the outermost access points of the human life—by which discernment, instruction, and subtle discrimination take in influences, and by which formed thoughts are expressed. Many sentinels are set over the outer faculties to protect their admissions; and the directive faculty within can open or shut them as it wills.

But when enlightened understanding from Divine truth approaches to enter these outer admissions, the governing falsities and evils that serve as gatekeepers resist and restrain it, binding the whole outer circuit so that what they treat as alien cannot enter.

Nevertheless, enlightened understanding by wisdom, reverence, and persistent effort subdues those guards and takes control of the restraints upon every admission. Then the admissions that were formerly opened to craving—admitting delighting appearances, transmitting speech devoted to worldly entanglements, and admitting appropriations that mingle what is unclean with disorder—are no longer used for such reception; and when one ceases to admit these, the inner ruling love and thought follow after.

Thus, because the restraints upon the life of the regenerating person are held by enlightened understanding within, that person is opened to admit what accords with the Divine. Discernment is opened to receive perceptions of genuine love, justice, and righteousness. What formerly appeared attractive—splendid persuasive appearances crafted to adorn craving and excite self-love—no longer delights, because craving that once ruled has been humbled by the indwelling enlightened understanding. The displays of craving become repugnant, while the qualities of the Divine—justice, humility, and the preference for simple truths not fashioned for self-display—are pleasing.

Likewise the capacity for instruction, once open to vain teachings, seductive persuasions, and concealed practices of evil, now finds such things repugnant and refuses them. Instead it delights to receive teachings of justice, worshipful affections, elevations of prayer, acknowledgement of the Divine, instruction of truth, and understanding of charity.

In sum, the outer admissions that were once open to evil are now opened to good. Disorder once ruled the outward life without higher command; but now its authority in the outward faculties is taken away. Living good from the Divine becomes lord over all operations, binding them in stable harmony and confirming them in the certainty of truth. The outer admissions are opened to good; good enters by discernment and instruction and dwells in the central governing love. Divine influence reigns, annulling disorder as the initiating impulse and final purpose, and establishing instead the Divine willing and the intention of enlightened understanding.

Therefore, by wisdom and practiced skill, set firm protection around the outer circuit of life’s admissions, so that the disorder that still presses upon the outer level has no power to steal your understanding of truth, scatter it into conflicting states, or drive it through shifting distortions.

---

## Chapter 57: Concerning the Generation of Adam.

**¶2** Now, no[te] that Adam is mighty in his stature, and great is his size. Even his years are many! He spent many years living in the world. And he is not the only one we have found to be mighty; but all the others who were begotten in his generation, many are their years too. Still, these people who are born today, their stature has been diminished in power; even their years are lessened. Also, why is the birth today, of [the]y who are born, altered compared with that of these first [ones, and they did not change from] place to place? See, even the stars and signs of the zodiac continue in their positions. Why now have the age and the years of these last ones diminished, and he has also become smaller, compared to these ancient ones who belong to these first generations?

**¶3** [*] They increased, have turned, and diminished.

**¶4** [*] So, mankind is begotten, and [the ani]mals. They are born in these powers. Therefore, because of this, the offspring born in their generations and their lifetime: their years were found to be greater. They attain a long life span in the generation of Adam, and [tha]t of Sethel his son, and that of the ones who came after him.

**¶5** After they ceased to reign, namely the powers who are masters over the years; the powers who are masters over the months themselves reigned. Like the months, for they are less than the years; again this [is] also the case with the offspring w[ho were] born in them. Their life span is less [than the life span of the ones born] in the generation of the powers who master the years!

**¶6** When the authority of the powers who master the months was completed; the powers of the days received authority after them. Therefore, like the days, for they are less than the months, this also is the case with the offspring who were born in them. Their life span is less than the life span of the ones born in the powers who are masters over the months. Again, even so the offspring who were born in the hours, and of the moments. Their {conduit} is less than that of the months and the days.

**¶9** Now, can you recount to me this other (matter): By what sort of birth was the entire offspring of mankind made from one man and one woman, Adam and Eve?

**¶10** For the manner of these first generations is that they are richer in their light and purer in stature. Even their years are greater. Thus, again, even their offspring is much more than the offspring of this tim[e]. They were spending a longer time [forming] in [the w]omb of their mother. Afterwards, they [brought forth] a number in a single womb. There are times five were brought forth from a single womb, and times six were brought forth from [a] single womb! There are some lesser than this; again, some greater than this. Indeed, because of this the offspring of Eve multiplied, and of her children. The earth was filled by reason of them alone; because the offspring from the generation of the people of old, who they bore into the world, differ greatly from those of today. Their conception and their birth is not like latter births, brought forth nowadays from the woman's uterus.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶2 [none]: Coherent correspondential opening: a functional question about why early humanity was larger/longer-lived though the cosmic order (stars/zodiac) remains fixed. No textual seam or foreign voice is requ
> - ¶3 [excise]: Insertion of administrative-astrological taxonomy into an otherwise functional inquiry. The paragraph shifts from ‘why decline?’ to a fivefold bureaucracy of ‘masters’ and ranked authority, with named
> - ¶4 [excise]: Mixed paragraph: it begins in administrative voice (authority-from-beginning, powers reigning), but then reattaches to the chapter’s functional question about lifespan in early generations. The admini
> - ¶5-¶6 [annotate]: Continuation of the same administrative time-bureaucracy (year→month→day→hour/moment) used to explain diminishing lifespans. The sequence is internally consistent, but it remains governance-by-success
> - ¶9 [none]: Clean seam and genuine topic-shift marker, not an insertion: the text openly signals a new question (generation from one pair). This is a normal didactic transition and does not introduce alien admini
> - ¶10 [none]: Coherent correspondential generative teaching using embodied process (gestation, multiple births) as the functional basis for explaining early abundance and later reduction. No narrative break; the bo

**Changes:** Applied excisions per the critic’s recommendations: (1) Finding 2 (¶3) excise—removed the administrative-astrological fivefold ‘masters’ taxonomy; retained only the opening clause so the paragraph remains grammatical and coherent. (2) Finding 3 (¶4) excise—removed the specified governance framing (“And, thus, these powers have received authority… reigned”), keeping the Adam/Sethel lifespan application. No other paragraphs were altered; Findings 1, 5, 6 recommended none, and Finding 4 recommended annotation only (no textual change).

**Spiritual narrative:** Understand that the first heavenly human state possessed a far greater fullness of reception and a more powerful completeness of life, and that its states of wisdom were extended and manifold. It was not alone in this; those who proceeded from that first state shared the same largeness of capacity and length of spiritual endurance. But as successive states arose, the power of reception in those who later came forth was reduced, and the continuance of their states was shortened. The order by which good and truth are received had not itself been displaced, nor had the universal framework of arranged knowledges in externals been moved from its fixed service; yet the later human state became weaker and briefer than the earlier state.

This change occurred because the governing determinations that measure and distribute the human state are administered through a sufficient series of ruling agencies set over the ordered arrangement of external knowledges and the higher plane that flows into them. These administrations are distinguished as five successive modes of ruling: the widest mode that comprehends an entire cycle, the mode that divides that cycle into lesser cycles, the mode that determines particular states within those, the mode that defines still more specific variations, and the mode that fixes the least distinguishable changes. These five modes are permanent in the structure of the external and inner planes. Each mode has its own power, and a ruling agency is established over each. One administration presides over the whole cycle, another over the divided cycles, another over the particular states, another over their finer variations, and another over the least moments of change. These rule in order, the higher setting bounds for the lower, and the lower executing within the limits of the higher.

According to these administrations, spiritual life comes forth in human beings and also in their lower affections. Their first emergence is under one or another of these ruling determinations. For this reason these administrations have exercised authority from the first setting in order of spiritual life even to its last state. In the earliest states, the administrations that presided over the widest cycles were dominant. Therefore those who came forth under that governance possessed a more extended continuance of state, and their endurance in wisdom was found to be greater. A long spiritual life is perceived in the first heavenly humanity, and in the truth that proceeded from it, and in those who followed in that early succession.

When the dominance of the administrations over the widest cycles ended, the administrations over the divided cycles came into rule. And because those cycles are shorter than the whole, those who came forth under this governance had a briefer continuance of state. Their endurance was less than the endurance of those who belonged to the earlier governance.

When the governance of the divided cycles was fulfilled, the administrations of particular states received authority after them. And because particular states are briefer than the divided cycles, so also the continuance of those who came forth under them was shorter than the continuance of those who belonged to the governance of the divided cycles. Likewise, those who came forth under the governance of finer variations, and under the least moments of change, had still less continuance; the measure and transmission of their life was narrower than that of the broader administrations.

Consider further how an entire derived humanity could proceed from a single origin of good and a single origin of truth united as one.

The character of the earliest states was that they were richer in wisdom and purer in the form of life, and their continuance was greater. Consequently, what proceeded from them was far more abundant than what proceeds from later states. For a longer time the inward formation of new life remained within the protecting interior, until it was sufficiently organized for outward manifestation. Afterward, within one and the same interior reception, many distinct beginnings could be formed together. At times a full complement of new determinations would come forth from a single interior reception, and at times a complement still more abundant; in other cases less, and in other cases greater. By this abundance the derivations from the first truth-bearing receptacle were multiplied, and from them further derivations. The ultimate plane of life was filled by them alone, because the proceeders from the ancient state differed greatly from those of the later state. The way they received influx and brought it forth into outward life was not like the later way of producing new states in the external human.

## Chapter 58: The Four Powers that grieve

**¶2** ⟨p.147⟩ The first who grieves is the first Mother of Life. She is sad because of her children who are set in affliction, for they were conjoined with the Darkness and the [poison]son. They have been bound with the entire ruling-power. For what was she grieving, [watching]tching over them taking these afflictions? Whenever she might [see]ee them and how suffering is brought upon them, she shall grieve and be unhappy on account of them.

**¶3** ⟨p.148⟩ [The]econd one who is troubled of heart is the First Man, whose [greatness]ness and kingdom is established in the li[light] ship [of his Father]. And he too is troubled of heart because of his five sons who are set in the midst of danger. For any time he may see that they are given trouble, always he might hear the sound of their weeping as they cry out because of the blows and wounds of the enemy who assaults them daily; and as he sees them like this constantly, he shall g[grie]eve and mourn on account of his [s]o[n]s.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶2 [none]: Clean correspondential substrate: grief is taught by function (vigilant perception of the children’s affliction) and the narrative logic is continuous within the paragraph.
> - ¶2 ("poison son") [none]: Genuine difficulty from damaged/unclear lexeme rather than insertion: a corrupt or uncertain word-form forces interpretive smoothing, but it does not introduce a new administrative register or break t
> - ¶3 [none]: Clean correspondential substrate with minor local damage: grief is grounded in the First Man’s functional relation to his sons (he perceives their danger, hears their cries, sees their wounds). The on
> - ¶3 ("light ship of his Father") [none]: Genuine difficulty (possibly idiom, loanword, or corrupt reading) that the reader paraphrases to preserve sense; not a seam-expansion or administrative insertion.

**Changes:** No changes made. All findings (1–4) recommended none (no excision or annotation), including the difficult lexemes "[poison]son" (Finding 2) and "li[light] ship [of his Father]" (Finding 4), which were judged genuine difficulties rather than intrusive expansions.

**Spiritual narrative:** The first to be in grief is the primal wisdom of love. It is distressed because the goods and truths that proceed from it are held in oppression, having joined themselves to denial of good and to destructive falsity. They are confined under the full dominance of that ruling evil. Therefore wisdom remains in vigilant care over their state and is pained: whenever it perceives how suffering is being imposed on them, it is troubled and saddened for them.

The second to be troubled inwardly is the primary good that first confronts evil, whose full power and ordered domain are fixed in wisdom from the Divine. It too is troubled because the sufficient complement of its operative powers are placed in the midst of severe spiritual trial. For whenever it perceives them being harassed, it is continually aware of their lamentation as they cry out under the injuries inflicted by opposing evil and falsity that assail them without ceasing; and seeing this unremittingly, that primary good grieves and mourns for its powers.

## Chapter 59: The Chapter of the Elements that wept

**¶2** The garments, the sons of the First Man, wept bitterly three times. Then they were silent. There is a fourth time of weeping, for they will weep at the end. And then their crying can stop, and they will never again weep from this time on.

**¶3** Now, the first time of their weeping is when they had seen the darkness, the enemy being about to burst out against them. They understood everything that would happen to them. They were aware of the one that would rise and come upon them, but by necessity they hastened to him and fought with him. They mastered him, they detained him. They bound him with their body and their limbs, they overcame his death and his fire and his darkness.

**¶4** [The] second time that they wept is [when the]ir father, the First Man, [ascend]ed from the [abyss] of darkness and left them behind; that is the time when he stripped [them off] and left them below. Indeed, when th[ey] s[aw] how the First Man had risen out of the abyss and [l]eft them behind, and that they were abandoned in the enmit[y, th]ey wept and were sore of heart. Still, what could they do, wishing [to subject] their heart to every affliction. They [remained b]ehind and grew [s]ick under the pressure of the weighty burden of all the works, until the end time.

**¶5** [The] third time that they wept is when the Living Spirit stripped off the three garments of activity, placing them beneath all things. He gave them a sign to lift their heads and rise to the heights from that place; and to pass and journey through all the earths until they should reach the land of tranquilli[t]y and peace, the place of rest prepared for them. So, when they had seen him, that he had separated himself from them, while they themselves remained behind after him and had not ascended; at that moment when they stayed behind these three garments wept. After their crying, they turned their [hear]t to all the heights that were concealed from them, and were of one mind. For [they would] soar up and come to the heights; and pass over all things until the time that was ordained for them all [and be gathered up] at the end of the worlds.

**¶6** [The f]ourth time when they will weep is when the [S]tatue will be taken up on the last day, and they will weep [for] the souls of the liars and blasphemers; for they may give [no help to them] because their limbs have been severed [from the bonds] of the darkness. And also [th]ose souls, when the Statue will go up and th]ey are left alo[n]e, they will weep in that they will rema[in] behind in affliction for ever. For they wi[ll be cut off] and separated from the Last Statue. An[d it is] a necessity to take these souls who are ready for loss as retribution for the deeds that they have done. They go i[n to] this [darkne]ss and are bound with the darkness; just as they desired it and loved it, and placed their treasure with it. At that very moment, when the Last Statue rises up, the[y wi]ll weep. And then they will scream out loud because they will be severed from the company of this great Statue. And they remain behind for ever. This great weeping [before the rulers] is terrible, it occurs in front of the souls and [the powers] who are ready for loss in accordance with their deeds. And they [will] weep with that crying, they will never be silent; because they will not attain rest from this time on.

**¶7** So, they too are the garments of these elements. This fourth time of weeping is heart-rending for them. It is much worse for them than these other three times of weeping before. This fourth time the weeping is not for themselves. Rather they weep and are heartsore for these soul[s] who will be cut off from peace, because rest will not come about for them, to allow them to rest from torment for ever.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶2 [none]: Clean correspondential framing of a fourfold sequence of states (three present griefs + one eschatological grief) with a functional reason for cessation. No narrative break; this paragraph supplies th
> - ¶3 [none]: Clean correspondential combat scene: perception → necessary engagement → restraint/binding → named effects (death/fire/darkness) as functional qualities. No administrative intrusion (no ranks, station
> - ¶4 [none]: Clean correspondential separation-and-burden sequence. The physical ‘stripping’ imagery is continuous with the functional narrative (withdrawal of the First Man; the garments left below; endurance und
> - ¶5 [none]: Mostly clean correspondential teaching, but with a localized lexical/imagistic strain that likely reflects either translation tradition or doctrinal accommodation rather than an administrative inserti
> - ¶6 [none]: Genuine difficulty concentrated in the term ‘Statue’ and the sudden widening from the garments’ own process to a public eschatological tribunal. However, the passage is not administrative in Mani’s se
> - ¶7 [none]: Clean closing rationale that ties the ‘elements/garments’ back to the fourfold weeping structure and clarifies the fourth grief’s object (for the lost, not themselves). This resolves rather than disru

**Changes:** No changes made. All findings (¶2–¶7) carried a recommendation of none; there were no excise targets to execute.

**Spiritual narrative:** A sufficient complement of operative powers invested with external truths underwent a full series of grief in the ultimate degree three times, and then came into restraint and waiting. Yet there remains a final grief at the consummation, after which their lamentation in the ultimate degree ceases, because their work there is finished and no such distress can return.

The first grief came when those external truths first perceived active falsity, and saw it about to break forth against their domain. From that perception they comprehended the whole sequence of what would befall them. They recognized the evil that would be raised up to assault them, and yet by the necessity of order they were brought into direct engagement with it and contended against it. They prevailed over it and held it in restraint. They fixed it in the ultimate degree through the very forms and powers by which they act there, and so they subdued the intention of spiritual death, the ardor of self-love, and the obscurity of falsity that accompanied it.

The second grief came when the first good that had been set for combat was elevated from the ultimate obscurity and withdrawn upward, and those external truths were left in the lower state; this was the state in which their outward investment was removed and they were left to remain in what is beneath. When they perceived that the leading good had been raised out of ultimate falsity and had left them behind, and that they were abandoned amid hostility, they fell into grief and interior pain. Yet they could do nothing but endure, because their willing was turned toward every permitted suffering for the sake of the whole work. Therefore they remained behind and languished under the pressure of the heavy burden of all effects and labors required in the lowest degree, even until the final time.

The third grief came when the operation of Divine truth from the Divine removed a full series of outward powers of action, and arranged them beneath all things in the ultimate plane for service there. It gave them assurance and urging to elevate their understanding and to be raised into higher states from that low condition, and to progress through every lower degree of life until they should come to tranquility and peace, the rest prepared for them. When they perceived that this higher operation had separated itself from their immediate state while they themselves still remained below and had not yet been elevated, then those outward powers of action fell into grief. After that lamentation, they turned their intention toward all the higher states still hidden from them, and they became of one understanding and purpose: that they would be lifted into higher states and advance beyond all lower things, until the time appointed for their full elevation at the close of the ages.

The fourth grief will occur at the last judgment, when the final collective form of what has been gathered for elevation is taken up into complete separation from the lower. Then there will be lamentation on account of those who have confirmed falsity and profaned truth, because they can no longer receive restoration, since the operative powers by which conjunction could have been effected have been torn away from the influence of falsity. Those same souls, when that collected form is elevated and they are left alone, will lament because they remain behind in distress forever. For they are cut off and separated from that final collective, and it is necessary that those who have made themselves ready for ruin be taken into their recompense according to their deeds. They enter into falsity and are held in it, because they desired it and loved it, and placed their inmost delight and confidence within it. At the moment of the final elevation, they lament; and then their lamentation becomes vehement, because they are severed from the fellowship of that great collected form. They remain behind forever. This lamentation is dreadful and public among those fixed for ruin according to their works, and it does not cease, because they will not attain rest from that time onward.

For these external truths are also the outward investments of the fundamental evils and falsities in the ultimate degree, and this last lamentation rends them more than the three earlier griefs. It is worse than the former states, because this final lamentation is not for their own elevation, but for those souls who are cut off from peace, since no rest can come to relieve them from torment to eternity.

---

## Chapter 60: Concerning the Four Fathers; what they are like.

**¶1** Just as the corporeal body has limbs that are hidden, so it also has others that are visible. They can be seen upon it, they can be grasped and receive blows and wounds, whilst others can neither be grasped nor receive blows.

**¶2** So it is also with this entire greatness, as we resemble it. Some of it is hidden, whilst some of it is visible; some of it is far away from the blows and suffering, whilst some of it receives blows and wounds.

**¶3** Now, thus the directive of the body that lives within, hidden in the storehouses inside the body and unseen. So it is also how the Father, the God of truth, exists; as he too is hidden in his kingdom, not displayed before this outer desolation.

**¶4** Thus, too, the body has two eyes that are visible and seen, and are even the body's source of illumination; so there are also these two light ships that shine and are seen in the entire universe and the whole of creation. The light has never been hidden, just like the two eyes of the body.

**¶5** The living soul too is like this; it is established in apparent silence. In its apparent silence it is grasped and receives blows from these five fleshes, which destroy (the soul) and strike it. It can be likened to the mystery of the corporeal body, as it can be grasped and mastered, can receive blows and wounds.

**¶6** ⟨p.152⟩ Also, thus this tongue that speaks, proclaims, reprimands and reveals about everything happening in this body, inside and outside, also the considerations and bodily knowledges. The tongue, which is outside, makes it manifest. It also proclaims about the honour and shame of the body. And it speaks, unveiling the pain of the body that is sick from its sores.

**¶7** [*] 

**¶8** [*]

> - ¶3 [none]: Clean correspondential identification of the First Father as ‘directive of the body that lives within’: the Father corresponds by *hidden interior governance* (life-directive in storehouses) rather th

> - ¶5 [none]: Genuine difficulty within a coherent correspondential move: the paragraph shifts from ‘visible/hidden organs’ to the soul’s vulnerability and introduces a fixed Manichaean-sounding numeral phrase (“fi
> - ¶6 [none]: Clean correspondential development: ‘tongue’ is presented as an outward faculty whose *function* is disclosure/reproof, matching the chapter’s theme of visible/outward mediation. No named authorities,

**Changes:** Applied Finding 6 (Recommendation: excise): removed ¶7–8 in full as a seam-expansion with christological naming and ‘sent’ formula overlaying the functional correspondence. No other paragraphs were modified (Findings 1–5: none/annotate only).

**Spiritual narrative:** As in a person there are inner powers that cannot be directly seized or harmed, and outer functions that can be handled and therefore struck, so in the whole ordered Divine reality there is what remains withdrawn in interior holiness and what stands forth in outward operation. Some Divine things abide beyond assault, and some enter the level where conflict and injury can be felt.

In the same way, the ruling life within a person is held in the deep interiors of the mind and is not exposed to the senses; so the Divine of truth abides concealed in His own interior kingdom and is not put on display before the desolate outer state.

Yet there is also a constant power of discernment that stands openly present and gives illumination, and therefore a paired, steady disclosure of wisdom shines throughout all lower orders and all effects. Wisdom in its enlightening office is not withdrawn, just as discernment in its outward act is not hidden.

But the living human spirit, while it rests in a state that seems quiet and unresisting, is nevertheless seized at the outward level and struck by a sufficient set of bodily cravings and natural compulsions that batter it and work to ruin it. In that outer degree it can be constrained and dominated, and so it suffers injury.

There is also an outward faculty of utterance and doctrine that announces, corrects, and makes known everything occurring in a person, inwardly and outwardly, including the mind’s reflections and the body’s learned impressions. This outward expression makes conditions manifest, declaring what is noble and what is disgraceful in the life, and uncovering the distress of a life made sick by its evils.

So too the Divine revealer who proceeds from the highest Divine good makes all things known: outer and inner, higher and lower. He discloses even the interior heavens that are not exposed to outward sight, and he discloses the openly shining forms of wisdom that are perceived. He lays open their glory and the Divine good that dwells within them.

And concerning the injuries and assaults suffered by the living spirit, he is the one who brings them to light. He also teaches about its rest, and he discloses the way its stains are removed and its soundness restored.

---

## Chapter 61: Concerning the Garment of the Waters: how great is its Measure.

**¶6** [At] the time when the First Man assumed the elements, and stood [fi]rm against the first enmity that had come about in the darkness, he defied these great and powerful rulers. They are immeasurable, greater than the extent or size of thousands and ten thousands of parasangs. At the start, when he spread the fight against them, he gave them fire and wind; and afterwards he gave the garment of the waters to the rulers of death. So they, the rulers, drank in and consumed those waters, [the way that I told] you [about it]. Many [waters] poured forth from the garment of waters [and from them] the rivers flow. The mouth of the rulers [received them] those [is not filled by], the warriors of destruction [and all] laboured and brought them forth.

**¶7** There were seven (rivers), like the great river Tigris [that you] see, flowing in to the mouth of each one of them, as they shall not [be gathered] to a single stomach, to their fill; because in turn the descent to its stomach shall burn them with its fire and heat. Those rulers were like the [images] of [deception]; they were not emptied; rain and [mist] their existent days.

**¶9** [This] is how great a struggle, since it has [revealed] these creatures of destruction, the mighty giants. Behold, they are entwined, bound, and [fixed] in every place. They are hidden, not revealed before the eyes of every flesh that exists on the land of death [but they are in their place]. These rulers [also shall be] revealed. The entire universe of flesh will be destroyed and dissolved [in that day]; all pass away [in a moment]. They will be destroyed with their angry voice, which is mightier than the thunder.

**¶10** Now, as this small stream is reckoned [as nothing] before the vastness of these waters of which I have recounted to you. For only one river from thousands of rivers of water, from the secrets that are not revealed, is the river Tigris upon the earth.

**¶11** See [at these waters] and be amazed at the measure of the elements of the waters. Marvel at the stature of the Man who bore them. And understand how great is that god, active in power, the one whom these waters [are greater] than the stature of the First Man who [assumed] this garment of such size; and how much was made.

> - ¶9 [none]: Clean correspondential unit: it shifts from hydraulic mechanics to visibility/constraint and eschatological disclosure, but the shift is thematically motivated (what is hidden now will be revealed; th
