**¶23** Also, the teaching of wisdom, which circulates in that body: to the likeness of the Virgin of Light, who goes up and comes down in this zone, above and below.

**¶24** Also, love and joy, faith and truth, in which the person should live: they are to the likeness of these two light ships. For, the living soul should go up in them and become free through them; and it ascends from the abysses below and arrives at the heights above.

**¶25** Once more, understand this other (truth): There exist four worlds in this body of the flesh, and there exist countless times seven rulers in the body of the four worlds!

**¶26** So, the first world is from its neck up; and also the seven rulers who are in this upper world are these: the two organs of sight, and the two of hearing, and the two of smell. The other one is the mouth, which is the organ of taste.

**¶27** The second world is from its neck down to its entire upper torso. And also, the seven rulers who are in this second world are: its two arms that accord to the pattern of its two ears, which occur in its first world above; and its two breasts that are to the pattern of its organs of smell; and the two eyes of its heart; and the gullet of the stomach between the two breasts, which in the chest is the pattern of the mouth.

**¶28** ⟨p.173⟩
Once more, there exist seven rulers in the third world of the body, which are these: the fat, the lung, the spleen, the liver, the gall, and its two kidneys.

**¶29** Also, the fourth world below. There are another seven there of this same kind: its two buttocks (?), and its two testicles, and its two loins; and the member out of which the seed comes, that begets all the forms.

**¶31** Aries and Taurus, Gemini, Cancer, Leo, Virgo, Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius, Capricorn, Aquarius and Pisces; these are the twelve signs of the zodiac that occur in the worlds above, on the sphere. They are interlocked and bound on this turning wheel, the one in which they are [set], fixed, and planted. And, regarding all these twelve signs of the zodiac, this is how they were appointed and made leaders [set] on the sphere beneath the heaven that is the wheel of the stars. Now, they are appointed like this, they are thus, so that you can find the head of [upon the twelve stations] and you find the tail too [each sign, and its companion]

**¶32** Again, they occur like this, these that we have recited, one after another in this body. They are counted by order and number from the head to the feet. Its head is Aries. Its neck and its shoulders are Taurus. Its two arms Gemini. Its upper torso is Cancer. Its stomach is Leo. Its belly is Virgo. The vertical spine and its intestines (?) are Libra. Its genitals Scorpio. Its loins are Sagittarius. Its knees are Capricorn. Its shinbones are Aquarius. The soles of its feet are Pisces.

**¶33** Behold, these are also distributed one after another. They exist in this body, as if turned to the side and bent to the pattern of the twelve signs of the zodiac. They too are thus, arranged one against another, head to tail, as they occur on the wheel [as it rises] up.

**¶34** Also, they are counted by order and number of the body and all its members. So, we have proclaimed that these are turned to the side, bent, and spread out; because from its head down to its hip shall count six to its left, and another six to its right.

**¶35** ⟨p.174⟩
The six that are down its right are these that I will recount: the right temple is Aries; its right shoulder is Taurus; its right arm is Gemini; its right rib-cage is Cancer; its stomach is Leo; the right side of its reproductive organs are Virgo.

**¶36** Conversely, the other six about which we have spoken, which are to its left, in contrast they come from its hip to its head: its left [hip] is Libra; its left rib-cage is Scorpio; its left breast and left kidney is Sagittarius; its [left] elbow is Capricorn; its left shoulder is Aquarius; its left temple is Pisces. This is how the creator of the body himself has appointed them. He has [set] them in order and arranged them, one against its companion, head to tail.

**¶38** Listen to this other lesson that I will proclaim to you. Understand that there exist many powers in this body. They are the house-dwellers who are made the leaders in it. There are eight hundred and forty times ten thousand rulers made chiefs in the human body! They are distributed and set firm, quartered according to house. The count and number of their houses is two hundred and ten times ten thousand.

**¶39** When all these rulers come creeping and moving within the body, they will meet one another; and they shall beset and destroy one another, and [work destruction] in them there. When [when the time is fulfilled]] they shall erupt from the body of the person who [will] die; and make putrid boils and sores and burning wounds in the body. Either they might have made him sick within; or else they might come forth upon his outer side. Those wounds shall first be squeezed and discharge their liquid, and empty out their pus, until they are squeezed [again, and again] and the suppuration forced out from the wound. Afterwards, their scars shall close over; and they are healed and obtain relief from the places that came out upon them.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶2 [none]: Clean correspondential opening: macrocosm–microcosm thesis stated without governance inventory or rank-listing.
> - ¶3-4 [none]: Clean correspondential anatomy-by-function mapping; difficulties are genuine (minor textual/lexical uncertainty), not insertion.
> - ¶5-16 [excise]: Insertion of an administrative governance dossier into a correspondential body–cosmos mapping; narrative voice switches from functional likeness to jurisdictional chain-of-command with named masters a
> - ¶23-24 [none]: Clean correspondential resumption after the administrative block: returns to ‘circulates in that body’ and paired inner states as vehicles, i.e., functional teaching rather than jurisdictions.
> - ¶25-29 [none]: Mostly correspondential but with a mild mixed didactic: ‘worlds’ and ‘rulers’ language could be read administratively; however the rulers are immediately given as bodily faculties/organs, so the spiri
> - ¶31-36 [none]: Genuine difficulty from imported astral nomenclature, but not an administrative overlay: it is still correspondential mapping (zodiac ↔ body), organized by order/number and by successive/simultaneous 
> - ¶38-39 [none]: Clean correspondential/physiological moralization: ‘powers’ are described by behavior (creeping, meeting, destroying, erupting) and bodily consequence (sores, discharge, healing), not by jurisdiction.

**Changes:** Per Finding 3 (¶5–16) recommending excision of the administrative governance dossier (five camps/watch-stations and their afflictions), paragraphs ¶5 through ¶16 were removed in full. All other paragraphs were left unchanged per the remaining findings (Recommendations: none).

**Spiritual narrative:** All things in the whole expanse of being, in higher and lower degrees, are set in one order after the form of the Divine Human; and the formed outer person is an effect of that same order brought down into use.

The first receptacle of life in a person is as an initial gathering of fundamental truths prepared for manifestation. From the first descent of life into what begins to govern thought and intention down to the inmost center where love rules, there is the likeness of the whole arranged series of interior degrees. The ruling love itself is the central turning by which all subordinate things are held together and kept in ordered motion. From that ruling center down to the lower powers that receive, sort, and separate, there is the likeness of the intermediate region that mediates between inward and outward, extending from higher understanding into the lowest life. The active outmost power is the ultimate plane where determination passes into effect. From the lower powers of reception down to the strength of natural life there is the likeness of the complete outmost planes in their threefold arrangement. The hardened supports of action are the ultimate station where what is lowest is held fast under a guarding authority. The final sensibility that touches the world is the lowest plane itself, together with the fixed restraints that prevent it from breaking order. Ardent desire is the containing form of love; obscured corporeal sensation is the containing form of falsity that extinguishes light; and the circulating natural life is the containing form of truth in the understanding, living or perverted according to the ruling love.

In this way the lesser form is an image of the greater in its interior degrees, in its ordered arrangements, in its elevations and descents of state, in its boundaries, and in its containing forms.

There are five general administrations in the outermost region of ordered life, and a sufficient complement of operative powers preside over them.

In the first and highest administration, governed by the preserving of brightness in understanding, there is authority over the three discrete interior degrees; and beneath it stand subordinate powers in service under the governance of the upholding of the dignity of good.

In the second administration, governed by the upholding of the dignity of good, there is authority over the complete series of the arrangements beneath.

In the third administration, governed by the power that gives firmness and resistance against evil, authority extends from the intermediate region down into the lowest plane; and according to its office it strengthens the general ordering of thought that turns through knowledges, and the breadth of intermediate life, together with a complete set of outward arrangements appointed within the natural degree.

In the fourth administration, governed by the guarding of acknowledgment of what is Divine, there is authority over the three general turnings of life’s operations, and over the three outmost planes borne upon the ultimate guarding power.

In the fifth administration, governed by the ultimate guarding power, there is authority over the lowest plane on which it is fixed, and over the fixed restraints by which that plane is held together.

Yet disorder has entered these administrations at times.

In the administration of preserved brightness, evil pressed upward against a formed receptacle of higher message; it was barred from entrance into that degree and fell back, stripped of confidence.

In the administration of upheld good, a shaking of foundations and betrayal arose; but the powers that stood up in that moment, and the powers sent to restrain, were brought down until they were reduced into submission.

In the administration of firmness against evil, aborted beginnings of life descended into the lowest plane and fashioned an external humanity without inward order.

In the administration of guarded acknowledgment, a tumult arose in the outmost planes above the ultimate guard, and the way of elevation was obstructed for the three general operations by which life ought to rise—those of persuasion, of understanding, and of love.

In the administration of the ultimate guard, the fixed restraints beneath were uncovered and the bindings of order were strained, and a great overthrow came upon the ultimate plane.

Further, the instruction of wisdom that circulates through a person is the image of pure receptivity of truth from the Divine, which in ordered alternation descends into lower degrees and ascends again through all degrees.

Likewise, love and delight, faith and truth, in which a person ought to live, are the two general means of elevation and liberation; by them the living mind is raised and set free, lifted from the lowest obscurities into the highest states.

There are four general regions of life in the outer person, and within the life of these four regions there are endlessly repeated complete sets of governing principles.

The first region is the highest natural plane, and its governors are the receptive faculties for perception and discernment in their paired forms, together with the faculty that admits and judges what is taken in for appropriation.

The second region is the plane of outward power for interaction, and its governors are the paired powers of operation answering to the paired powers of perception, the paired affections of nourishment and support answering to the paired discriminations, the two interior regards of the ruling love, and the faculty of admission and passage between them that answers to appropriation.

The third region has again a complete set of governors: the general provision for preservation, the power that breathes life into understanding, the power of discrimination and separation, the ardent desire that warms and excites action, the bitter reaction against evil, and the paired power of purification and balance.

The fourth region, the lowest, has likewise a complete set of the same kind: the paired supports for standing in the world, the paired potencies for producing effects, the paired strengths of conjunction, and the active power from which interior truth goes forth in potency to generate forms of life.

There is also a full organization of truths in the higher natural understanding, bound together in one turning order and fixed in their places, so that the whole may be led as one, and the beginning and the end may be grasped together within the same system.

This same full organization exists in a person successively from highest to lowest, so that each degree and function is under its leading truth, down to the outmost sensibility; and it exists simultaneously as well, distributed in mutual relation on both sides, set each opposite its companion and joined as beginning with end in a single form, according to the ordering of the maker of the human form.

Finally, there are innumerable lesser powers within a person, like inhabitants set as leaders within their own provinces. They are fixed in their quarters according to function, and the provinces of their functions exceed numbering. When these lesser powers move and operate, they meet; and if disorder rules, they oppose and ruin one another within their own domain. As outward life comes to its end, such disordered forces break out from the organized form and appear as grievous states in the natural degree—now as inward sickness, now as outward corruption. These states first open and cast out what has been stored up in falsity and evil until impurity is expelled from the affected place; afterward the outward breach is closed, and there is a temporary relief in externals from what had erupted, while the inner cause is healed only by reordering life from within.

---

## Chapter 71: Concerning the Gathering in of the [E]lements.

**¶2** [*] The light shall be gathered in to the fire, and the fire itself gathered in to the water, and the water gathered in to the wind, and the wind gathered to the air, and the air gathered to the answer, and the answer gathered to the summons. Also, the summons to the purified Mind, which is the intellectual. The intellectual to the Pillar of Glory, the aeons of greatness.

**¶3** They are gathered in like this; but, they will be gathered on one occasion only, and go up above to the place of rest for ever and ever.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶2 (opening chain: “light…fire…water…wind…air…answer…summons…purified Mind…intellectual”) [none]: Clean correspondential “return-chain” teaching: a functional sequence of transformations/assimilations culminating in rest. The reader’s only strain (“wind/air/answer/summons” needing contextual rende
> - ¶2 (bracketed clause: “[the Pillar of Glory to the First Man, the Man to the Ambassador, the Ambassador to] the aeons of greatness.”) [excise]: Insertion into a functional narrative: a named-agent ascent chain is spliced into an otherwise impersonal correspondential sequence. The bracketed run introduces administrative/personnel-style entitie
> - ¶3 [none]: Clean closure; it coherently seals the one-time consummation of the gathering and the resulting eternal rest. No voice conflict or narrative break.

**Changes:** Applied Finding 2 (¶2) excision: removed the bracketed named-figure ascent clause “the Pillar of Glory to the First Man, the Man to the Ambassador, the Ambassador to” to restore the impersonal correspondential return-chain. No other changes (Findings 1 and 3 recommend none).

**Spiritual narrative:** Wisdom is to be withdrawn into love; and love itself is to be withdrawn into the receptive understanding; and that understanding is to be withdrawn into a more interior power that impels and carries it; and that power is to be withdrawn into the encompassing general order that contains and arranges it; and that encompassing order is to be withdrawn into the clear perception that can respond from truth; and that response is to be withdrawn into the ruling influx that calls, disposes, and governs all things. This ruling influx is then to be directed into a mind made pure from falsity and disorder, which is the genuinely intellectual degree. The genuinely intellectual degree is to be drawn into the steadfast support of Divine manifestation; and that support into the first fully formed heavenly person; and that heavenly person into the mediating function that conjoins what is higher with what is lower; and that mediation into the whole expanse of the Divine realm in its greatness. This is the manner of their reunion; yet this complete reunion is to occur only once, and afterward all that has been gathered in this order is to be raised into an eternal peace, beyond further scattering or struggle.

---

## Chapter 72: Concerning the worn and torn apart Garments, or the tattered Clothes, or the Elements, the Cross, and the rest too.

**¶7** “It is proper for you to understand this. The garments that are named ‘great garment’ are the five intellectuals that make perfect the body of the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man. They were purified by the coming of the Ambassador.

**¶8** “In contrast, the ones named ‘rag’: the rags are the devoured enlightening power. It is compounded and entangled in the body of the universe in the rulers who are above, who exist in all the firmaments. Also, its shine is that which lights up and is displayed in the stars of the chain and the powers of the course. And likewise, these rags that are entangled in these three earths below, above the head of the Porter [at the foundation of all things] on the earth that is under the Porter. These are named the ‘rags’, because [it has been torn away from its own order,] set [and has been fastened in an outward damaged form]

**¶9** “However, the ‘great worn and torn apart garments’, which we have proclaimed, is this entangled living power. It is reaped, cut, and devoured. It is overpowered by the five worlds of the flesh, which creep as they move from place to place.

**¶10** “On the other hand, [these] we have termed the ‘images and the great tattered clothes’ [which we have proclaimed,] are the three great garments of the wind, the fire, and the water. These the Living Spirit cloaked upon his body. With them he set in order things in the universe. With them also he emptied the vessels. He swept them out and laid them bare in front of the Porter at the foundation of all things.

**¶11** “Conversely, ‘[the elements]ments’, we have called them this name, are this power that remains in all things below. It rises up in the wombs of all the earths, gathering together, and pouring forth upon all things.

**¶12** “And then, the ‘[Light] Cross’ [we have termed] this bound ‘light power’. It [is stretched out] all [things] upon the earth, in the dry and the moist.

**¶13** [*] “Also, it moves in all these powers that we have named. It awakens them, gives them ease, and drives them to their movement.

**¶14** “The one named ‘the soul that is slain’, which is killed, oppressed and murdered; it is this power of fruit, vegetables and seeds. They shall be threshed, plucked and cut down; and they give nourishment to the worlds of the flesh. Further: the wood should it be dry, and the garment should it become ragged, will perish. Again, it is [a part]rt of this entire soul that is slain and killed; since also even this whole part of it they kill [as they also kill it]

**¶15** “For all these names [that we have proclaimed] are a single [power,] since the beginning, it [was one power of light] as it enlightens; but they separated into all these parts in this first contest. They became set in all these altered forms, and these many names. Of course, if now all these varieties are laid bare, and stripped of all these appearances, and parted from all these names; they will gather together and make a single form, and a single name, unaltered and unchangeable for ever in the land of their first essence, from which they were sent forth against the enemy.”

> - ¶13 (remaining clause) [none]: After removing the institutional gloss, the remaining verbs can be read as correspondential (animation/relief/impulsion) describing how the named powers are activated. This looks like the substrate li

**Changes:** Implemented Finding 7 (¶13) excision by removing the administrative definitional clause “Also, the one named the ‘counsel of life’ is the summons and the obedience.” Per Finding 8, retained the remaining functional animation statements (“It moves… awakens… gives… drives…”). No other paragraphs were modified (all other findings recommended none).

**Spiritual narrative:** You must take in the inner meaning of these things. What is called the supreme investing is the sufficient fullness of ordered faculties of understanding that complete the integrated spiritual form of manifested glory, the fully formed heavenly human; and these faculties were cleansed and put into right order by the descent of a Divine mediation that brought purification into reception.

But what is called the torn investing is the enlightening power of understanding after it has been seized and consumed. It has been mixed and knotted into the outermost structure of the whole domain of lower life within dominating falsities and evils that rule in the higher external regions and extend through every successive level of outward consciousness. Its remaining brilliance is the scattered illumination that still flashes within the most external knowledges and within the regular operations of those outward forces. In the same way this seized illumination is also knotted into the lowest degrees, within the ultimate boundary where all things are held in check and weighed, and within the lowest natural level subject to that final restraint. It is called torn because it has been broken from its proper order and fixed in a damaged outward state.

What is called the greatly worn and torn investing is this same living illumination in bondage: it is gathered forcibly, divided, and consumed, and it is brought under the dominance of a sufficient series of merely bodily and sensual states that insinuate themselves and shift through outward forms, taking possession as they go.

And what is called the images and the great tattered investings are the full set of universal coverings of life in the lower degrees: the forms of love, the forms of truth, and the forms of their operation in outward life. The Living Divine invested these upon its manifested working, and by them arranged and stabilized everything in the whole domain of lower existence. By them also it cleared out the containing forms in the external region, purged them, and exposed their interiors at the ultimate threshold that stands at the base and support of all lower things.

What is called the fundamental principles is the same power of life that still remains in everything beneath. It rises up from the inmost receptacles of the lowest degrees, drawing itself together and then flowing out by influx into all things.

And what is called the Cross of Light is bound illumination held in restraint and extension, spread through the whole lowest domain, within states deprived of truth and within states that still receive truth.

And what is called the counsel of life is directing influx together with responsive reception. It moves within all these powers that have been named: it rouses them, grants them relief from pressure, and impels them into their proper activity.

What is called the soul that is slain—pressed down and destroyed—is the living power that should bring forth uses from inward truth: the capacity to produce works from perceptions, to generate beginnings of good and truth, and to sustain growth from them. These capacities are forcibly broken, uprooted, and cut off, and thus they become nourishment for merely bodily and sensual states. And when inward vitality is absent and outward truth has been ruined, what should have served life is lost; this too belongs to the same destroyed life, because even that whole portion is subjected to destruction.

For all these names are one in origin and one in their beginning: a single enlightening power as it shines forth. Yet in the first spiritual conflict it was divided into many parts, fixed into altered states, and so manifested as many appearances and many designations. But if these variations are uncovered and the outward show is removed, and the divided designations are put away, they will be gathered back into unity, forming one undivided state and one unchangeable identity forever within the plane of their first being, from which they first went forth to stand against what opposes the Divine.

## Chapter 73: Concerning the Envy of Matter

**¶1** [The] entirety of envy is this first nature that occu[red] in the worlds of darkness.

**¶2** In this envy that [was] in it, the First Man [received] its poiso[n] its evil-doing. His garment[s] were [rent;] it cut them and drew them; [it drew them down.]

**¶3** ⟨p.179⟩ It envied the first image of the Ambassador, which he displayed to it in the heights abov[e], i[n] his form that it saw. It established the seal and the image of flesh, and put the impression of t[h]at image on flesh, its very own birth.

**¶4** [The th]ird time it envied Adam, the fir[st hum]an. It seized a power from him, and bore it forth from a [rib of] of Adam's [flesh].

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶1 [none]: Clean correspondential opening-definition. Establishes envy by its origin and nature (what it is/does), not by a roster of rulers or jurisdictions.
> - ¶2 [none]: Clean correspondential depiction of envy’s action as poisoning, rending, cutting, and dragging down—i.e., a process described by effects and states. Named figure (“First Man”) functions as a narrative
> - ¶3 [none]: Still correspondential, but with a mild christological/institutional naming pressure point: “Ambassador” can be a later titular overlay for a function (revelatory mediation). Here it does not break th
> - ¶4 [none]: Genuine difficulty: the phrase “The third time” is an internal numeration that presupposes earlier enumerated envying events. In the present restored excerpt, only one prior explicit “envied …” is sta

**Changes:** No changes made. The critic recommended no excisions or annotations for ¶1–¶4 (Findings 1–4 all: recommendation “none”).

**Spiritual narrative:** The whole being of envy is the earliest turning of life against itself that arose where falsity had dominion and good was absent. Within that envy, the first good that goes forth to meet evil was made to drink the delight of falsity and to act from malice; the external truths that had clothed and protected that good were torn away, divided, and dragged down, so that the ultimate expression meant to guard inner life was bent into the service of what opposed it.

Envy then set itself against the first disclosed manifestation of the Divine message, as it was presented from a higher degree in a form it could apprehend. It stamped that manifestation with a mark of possession and fixed it into a merely outward, self-derived likeness; and it impressed this falsified likeness upon the lowest life it could seize, establishing it as an originating pattern within what it called its own propagation.

After this, envy turned again against the first genuinely human state. It tore away from that state a living capacity and brought it forth from an external truth joined to good in that life; and by separating it, envy made what should have served conjunction into a source for a derivative formation under its own rule.

---

## Chapter 74: Concerning the living Fire: It is present in Eight Places.

**¶2** The first place wherein it is, is this [light] land [where it is present and] exists. It is established beneath [it, and is]terly beyond the totality. It is the foundation and ba]sis of that great earth, and the consideratio[n that]at is beneath them all.

**¶3** The second place wherein it is, is the Father, the God of truth; because [the Fathe]r is established in his great, mighty consideration. So, [firs]t in the Father. The other powers that came from him were revealed through him, and (the fire) too came w[i]th them [and was revea]led by them.

**¶4** The third [place wherein it is], is the First Man, h[e] wh[who is clothed in] armour. Again, it is this fire [swathed and bound] on the body of the Perfect Man.

**¶5** [The fourth is this fir]e that was purified from all the [light] powers, [which is i]n the outer wall, in the ship of the day.

**¶6** [The fifth i]s this fire that is in the light-giver of the night [and is held] within the water.

**¶7** [*] [The] sixth is this f[i]re that is purified from the totality. The Living [Spirit] swathed it upon his body. He has established many things by it. He went down and stripped himself of it before the Por[t]er.

**¶8** The seventh is this living fire that is of the new earth, and is the consideration. For it is the first foundation in the new earth, it they built anew [and set in order] the first course, that is a crown for the entire earth. T[they] are below the totality, over the entire earth of da[rkne]ss [and they restrained] in it, in the [boundary] whole [of the]

**¶9** [The] eigh[th] is the fire that shall be revealed in this [last formed] person, and it signifies fear and war. Furthermore it [is fearful, and] it displays and manifests its sign in [its ter]ror. It shall be r[ev]ealed in these two light ships.

**¶10** This i[s the way] that the holy living fire exists in these [eight places].

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶2 [none]: Genuine difficulty from physical damage; no sign of a later administrative insertion. The correspondential teaching (place/ground/foundation) is present but the syntax is broken, which forced the spir
> - ¶3 [none]: Clean correspondential/substrate enumeration: the ‘place’ is defined by ontological procession (fire with the emanated powers), not by a ranked list of officials or jurisdictions.
> - ¶4 [none]: Clean correspondential teaching using functional embodiment imagery (fire ‘swathed and bound’ on the Perfect/First Man). This is not governance language; it is a state/garment correspondence.
> - ¶5-¶6 [none]: Coherent correspondential cosmological placements (day-ship, night-luminary, water) that still teach by function/medium, not by jurisdiction. Some obscurity is interpretive, not textual disruption.
> - ¶7 [excise]: Mixed: largely correspondential (fire as an operative ‘covering’ that enables establishing ‘many things’), but with a likely Mani-layer administrative/institutional overlay in the phrase “over the sum
> - ¶8 [none]: Genuine difficulty from damage at the end; otherwise coherent correspondential rebuilding/boundary imagery. No clear administrative roster or jurisdictional chain appears; the stumble is from fragment
> - ¶9-¶10 [none]: Clean closure to the eightfold enumeration; correspondential even where eschatological. ‘Fear and war’ functions as a state-signification, and ‘two light ships’ continues the earlier ship imagery rath

**Changes:** Applied Finding 5 (¶7) excision: removed the likely Mani-layer institutional overlay phrase “over the summons and the obedience,” leaving the surrounding garment/descent correspondence intact and minimally smoothing the sentence.

**Spiritual narrative:** Holy love is first found in the outermost recipient state in which it can appear and have being. There it is fixed beneath what transcends every finite grasp, and from that ultimate plane it becomes the base and support of an entire ordered system, together with the most general governing discernment that underlies all particular things.

Holy love is next found in the Divine source of truth, because that Divine is set in infinite and mighty wisdom. Therefore love is first in the Divine, and from that same Divine all subordinate powers went forth; and as those powers came forth, love went with them as their inner life and became manifest through their activity.

Holy love is next found in the primal heavenly human, the first fully formed reception of the Divine in a distinct state for protection and combat. In that perfected humanity, love is the very strength that invests and binds the whole of its embodied power.

Holy love is next found as love purified from every lesser admixture within the whole complex of enlightened powers, and fixed at the ultimate boundary of outward manifestation, within the orderly progression by which higher things are carried down into effect.

Holy love is next found in the lesser illumination that governs obscure states, and it is held within truth in a more external and reflective level of understanding.

Holy love is next found as love purified from all that is lower. The living Divine operation bound it to itself as a covering and instrument for action, to effect both the calling forth of lower states and their responsive submission. By this love many determinate forms were established; yet when descent to the lowest threshold was required, that operation laid aside this covering before entrance into the most external guardianship.

Holy love is next found as living love belonging to a renewed spiritual order, as the ruling wisdom within that renewal. It is the first basis of the renewed state; from it the whole arrangement was rebuilt, beginning with the first encompassing formation that completes and unites that order. This stands beneath what is beyond all, extending over the whole ultimate spread of falsity and evil and confining it within fixed limits, though the close of this description breaks into partial obscurity.

Holy love is finally found as love that will be made manifest in a last reception-state, and it bears the quality of dread and spiritual conflict. It makes itself known by the sign of its formidable presence, exposing and revealing by the awe it induces. It will be disclosed through a full conjunction of higher and lower manifestations of enlightened truth.

In this manner the holy living love has its existence through these eight degrees.

---

It did [not only report] in the answer that the First Man [was recounting] everything that had happened and everything [that would happen], all that had been done and whatever is made ready to be done by him. > - ¶2-¶3 [none]: Clean, coherent correspondential substrate teaching in epistolary form: a two-step process (summons → response) where the spiritual sense arises from what the ‘letters’ DO (convey peace/program; repor
> - ¶2 (final clause) [none]: Genuine difficulty from damaged/fragmentary text (lacuna) that forces interpretive smoothing; not evidence of later administrative insertion or seam-expansion.

**Spiritual narrative:** Attend to the first directed influx that proceeded from the Divine Life at the beginning. The Divine sent it into the good that first engages directly with evil. It was an impartation of concord and conjunction, flowing from love into the will as a salutation of unity to what was akin within the same order; and within that influx was contained, in full comprehensiveness, the whole disclosure of what was to be brought into effect, together with the entire arrangement of the uses it would establish through that influx. By that influx, the first good was set into its appointed course, and the whole process that must follow from it was determined.

In return, the responsive perception proceeding from that first good ascended into a more interior and elevated awareness, and so came back to the Divine Life. This, too, was a reciprocal communication, flowing from the engaged good back toward its source, and within it was comprehended the full disclosure of the state: every assault, every conflict, and every temptation that had been endured in opposing what was contrary. It carried the whole series in order—everything that had taken place in act, everything that was to take place in continuation, what had already been effected, and what was now disposed and prepared to be effected through that same good. Therefore the Divine Life, from its own omniscience and immediate presence, perceived completely all the oppositions, perils, and contests that the governing falsities had raised against that first good.

## Chapter 77: The Chapter of the Four Kingdoms

**¶2** ⟨p.189⟩ [The first is the kingdom of] the land of Ba[by]lon and of Persia.
The s[ec]ond is the kingdom of the Romans.
The thir[d is the k]ingdom of the Axumites.
The fourth is the kin[g]dom of Silis.
These four great kingdoms e[xi]st in the world; there is none that surpasses them.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶2 [annotate]: Clean, single-purpose classificatory notice: a bare fourfold list of world-kingdoms capped by a supremacy formula. The spiritual reading’s only “stumble” (the names are not spiritually functional) is 

**Changes:** No changes made. Finding 1 for ¶2 recommends annotation only (administrative four-kingdom inventory) and explicitly notes no internal seams or expansions warranting excision.

**Spiritual narrative:** There are four chief dominations in the outer life of the church and in the natural degree of the human spirit. The first is a rule that joins profaned holiness to persuasive reasoning, claiming what is sacred as its own authority while compelling the understanding to serve its reigning love. The second is a rule of external order and power, which governs by what is visible and worldly, binding minds into unity by outward compulsion rather than inward conviction. The third is a rule of more interior knowing that still operates chiefly in the outer degree, drawing strength from enlightened cognition and the zeal that proceeds from it, yet remaining a domination because it organizes many under one prevailing love. The fourth is a rule of fixed doctrine confirmed into rigidity, which secures itself by established forms and sets limits for thought and life according to what it has made authoritative. These four are the great general powers that stand forth in the natural plane; within that plane there is no broader set of ruling patterns by which communities are gathered and held.

> - ¶3 [none]: Genuine difficulty from damaged/duplicated wording, not a foreign voice. The paragraph is semantically consistent with the list, but the surface text shows copying/jogging errors (repetition, broken s
> - ¶4 [none]: Clean correspondential teaching: wealth is condemned specifically as ‘useless’ (non-functional), and pride is shown as the inner motion. The passage stays inside the fourfold vice-catalogue and contin

> - ¶6 [none]: Clean summarizing line that binds the four items into a universal/social propagation statement. Still correspondential: the emphasis is on human attachment and generative effort (“struggle so that the
> - ¶7 [none]: Potential seam marker, but not an administrative overlay. The ‘Judge of the worlds’ could be a later doctrinal titling, yet the sentence’s function is still correspondential: judgment is defined by se

**Changes:** No changes were made. All critic recommendations were “none” (Findings 1–7), with ¶3 noted as damaged but not an insertion and therefore not subject to excision.

**Spiritual narrative:** The first murderous ruling love is the craving to gratify the outer self, by which a person makes bodily delight the end, so that the understanding is dulled and the inward life falls into forgetfulness of higher things.

The second is the profanation of conjunction, when what ought to be a chaste union of good and truth is turned into unbridled and promiscuous mingling, so that the affection of union is defiled and mutual relation is reduced to appetite.

The third is the love of possession for its own sake, when a person gathers what has no use and calls it blessedness, taking pride in what is merely accumulated while removing it from any service to life.

The fourth is the love of dominion, from which come contentions, condemnations, and hostile judgments stirred up against one another; and when one will overcomes another, rule becomes tyranny, and renown in outward life is received as a reward.

All who attach themselves to these four universal ends—celebrated in common speech and common desire in the merely worldly mind—strain to bring them into effect among themselves and to multiply them in their societies.

But the Divine enters as judgment, setting discernment between opposites, and making an actual separation of living good and truth from deadening evil and falsity.

Therefore such corrupt ends do not remain joined to what is eternal; they are cast off, and the life that made them ruling comes to its end.

> - ¶5 [none]: Correspondential paragraph with localized damage/ellipsis. The ‘children of light’ phrase is not an administrative overlay here; it functions as a qualitative description of an uncorrupted state. The 
> - ¶6 [none]: Genuine difficulty from damaged text and compressed ritual language, producing an interpretive patch (‘sign upon the Cross’ → ‘fixed confession/rule’). This is not a clear administrative insertion; it

**Changes:** No changes made. All findings (¶3–¶6) carried a recommendation of none; therefore no excisions were executed, and no paragraphs were modified.

**Spiritual narrative:** The first practice is that a person willingly chastens the outer self by denying its cravings, and by this brings into obedience the whole governing love that had ruled within. The second is that the daily reception of life and understanding, as it is taken in through outward support and use, is to be consecrated and set in order, until it is made clean and sound, freed from the admixture of hellish evil and falsity that clings to it. The third is that the person is to make every action an act of holiness: whatever proceeds is to be in accord with the pure inner community of good and truth where there is no taint. One must not injure or profane what is received for life by bending it to corruption, but keep the inward state undefiled, so that the life within remains untroubled and at rest. The fourth is that one takes upon oneself a fixed confession of the Lord’s victorious endurance and, from it, a rule of restraint; one holds back one’s operative powers from destructive influence, so that no one violates the spiritual life in oneself or in another.

**Changes:** No changes made. All findings (1–6) recommended none; no excisions were justified.

**Spiritual narrative:** From zeal for what is right, the Divine brings whatever resists into the presence of truth, and that resistance at once loses its strength and is compelled into order under a just assessment.

The zeal you are permitted to have is not the delight of harming, but a righteous discernment that works for amendment. Toward those who are hard to lead, it presses for a change of mind. Its severity is chiefly a warning and a restraint, because it is not aimed at ruin but at turning back evil. The severity itself is the warning; and when the need is present, you may correct a companion with words that awaken fear, bringing him into a useful shame, so that stubborn resistance in his will may be removed and his interior intention may yield to what is right.

Yet there is another zeal that must be refused. It is the zeal that gives evil desire an opening and joins it with sin. It listens only to what flatters self, then burns from pride and self-display, fixing its attention on another in order to dominate. It does not speak what builds; instead it rages from arrogance and speaks from contempt. It defames to make the other seem worthless, refuses to acknowledge good, pours out abuse and condemnation, and longs to cast down what is gentle, repaying injury with injury and making retaliation its law. From this it either stirs others into wrongdoing, or, if someone falters, it seizes that fall as material for further condemnation and rejects any just evaluation. It withdraws into self-rule against the common understanding of companions; and when corrected, it converts that correction into a cause for rage and then commits evil from that rage. This is evil zeal, though it still takes the name of zeal.

A distinction must therefore be held between destructive rage and healing severity. When you must confront evil or stubborn falsity and someone hardens against instruction, you may see that he sinks into obstinacy and, from his own disorder, attributes harshness to you and returns harshness. Then what is required is not cruelty but firm, weighty truth that restrains and compels reflection. This should not be called rage, even if it is felt as painful; its true name is strong correction that aims at repentance.

The one who corrects rightly acts from understanding like a skillful healer whose perception is delicate and exact. The disorders to be removed may be severe, so the means are varied according to state. Sometimes what is needed is an ardent, searching truth that stings in order to cleanse; sometimes it is a gentle, soothing truth that binds and restores; and this is chosen according to the condition and according to what has preceded.

Thus there are some who accept sharp correction and are set right by it; and there are others who are harmed by sharpness and are restored only by humbling gentleness. The wise therefore adapts admonition to the state of the one being corrected, bringing down pride by truths fitted to its error until humility can acknowledge what is right. He also guards that his correction proceeds from what is godly within him so that his intention remains pure. He regards a companion in two modes: one by which he leads through love, and another by which he restrains through fear. For when stubborn resistance springs from foolish confidence and the delight of doing evil, fear can arrest it and so open a way to repentance. Yet even then the corrector remains ordered within himself, weighing words and their effect, and he persists until amendment is reached.

For the enlighteners in the higher heavens are manifested in these same two modes—love and fear—because their interior life flows through their whole being and presents itself fully. In the manifestation of love there is perceived beauty and elevation; in the manifestation of opposing presence there is perceived confrontation and restraint. By these two modes they cleanse and set in order the higher powers within those who receive their influence.

**Changes:** No changes made. All findings carried a 'none' recommendation (Findings 1–5), with ¶9–13 noted as damaged/garbled by transmission but not flagged for excision or alteration.

**Spiritual narrative:** Distinct and precious truths do not arise everywhere within the field of external knowledge, nor are they produced by that whole field taken generally. They come forth only in particular prepared states within that external domain, where an inflow of love from what is higher can be received. In those states that higher love kindles and flows downward, and its descent first produces a stirred and unstable outer condition, like an active but unclarified mixture in the external mind. Into that outer condition a gentle descending truth is introduced; it does not sink into the lowest and most general obscurity, but remains in a more accessible external level where it can be held.

That gentle truth is taken up by purer truths already present, and they unite. From this reception a first containing form is produced, and within it the inflowing truth is sheltered while it is ordered, purified, and made steady. If the incoming truth is not received as one coherent reception, but is divided into many small and scattered receptions, then it is fixed not as one complete precious truth but as many lesser precious truths, more or fewer according to the measure of conjunction and the state of reception.

But when the reception is whole and the containing form holds it without loss, a single precious truth is perfected in value according to the completeness of its conjunction. And even when reception begins in divided parts, if those parts are given time to adhere and mingle before any is dispersed, then a larger and more valued precious truth can still be perfected, because unity is formed within the containing form before scattering occurs.

Thus the formation of precious truths in the external degree proceeds in order: truth descends and is first received in an unsteady outward condition; from that first condition a containing form is brought forth; and that outward unsteadiness itself arises from the change and disturbance of the external mind as higher influx begins to arrange it.

When those who are in the work of restoration perceive such prepared states, they are moved toward them and enter into the more external and obscure levels where such precious things are hidden, and they raise them into clearer discernment. Each brings forth according to the measure of use and conjunction allotted to his function. What is recovered from obscurity is the same receivable truth that descended; those who recover it are the ministers of restoration; those who distribute it onward are enlighteners who give discernment; and those who finally receive it in higher order are the higher communities of greatness.

All who ascend within human natural life and are set free are brought back into those higher communities of light, and for them a state of rest is established there, within the higher order of greatness.

---

## Chapter 84: Concerning Wisdom; it is far superior when on the Tongue than in the Heart of the Person.

**¶1** Behold, when a person will be taught wise wisdom in his heart, and he seals it in his doctrine, so shall you find him rejoicing greatly about it. However, [sh]all his [joy be doubled] to him, more th[an] when he may pro-c[claim it] and utter it. ⟨p.205⟩ He shall be enlightened by it, and [t]hat [wisdom] shall shine forth the more in him. It is un-veil[ed be]fore him, and through it he assumes power and truth.

**¶2** I understand [indeed] that this word I have uttered is correct. I know that the wis-dom I have been taught is spread through my heart and perfected in my soul. (However), it is not found like the splendour in me, so that I regard it [ad]vanced, except when I shall proclaim it by my mouth and utter it to others. Indeed, when I proclaim it, I am giving it to the ears [of] others to hear. Would I do these same things, even if I had never heard it in [my] days of being? Would I desire greatly and my heart be drawn to the wis-dom I now proclaim? [I entreat y]ou, my master, that you might instruct me as to [w hy] this wisdom becomes more advanced when I proclaim it, than when it is sealed in my heart.

**¶3** Well [do] you ask! And great is this lesson for which you have sought, [that is] whence comes my great joy, on account of this wis-dom that I utter? [For this is why] it is superior in my mouth when I proclaim it than when it is set in m[y heart]. You yourself rejoice in it; and the other one who hears it from you, he shall rejoice in it, and be enlightened [by] it and receive thereby permanent strength.

**¶4** For like this matter, just so a small b[o]y who is conceived in the belly of his mother. He [and as he lives,] turns in his mother's womb, filling her womb. The mother knows and understands that this child she conceives is alive within her. She rejoices over him until [the tim]e when she gives birth. And he comes from her alive with his [limbs whole] and perfect in beauty, without defect, ⟨p.206⟩ [in] the living open air that is more sp[ac]ious than the first air he was in. He fills his eyes with the light and speaks with his living voice in the way of they who are born.

**¶5** Now, the time when this woman conceives the child in her belly, her joy at conceiving him in her womb is not so [ve]ry great as when she gives birth and sees him; and is full of his [be]auty and stature in the space of a single moment. The love and joy over him shall be a hundred [t]imes greater than it was, now that she has given birth and seen him.

**¶6** For the first time when she conceived him in her belly, his beauty and the sight of his eyes was hidden from his m[o th]er; but when she gave birth to him she saw his beauty. His s[t]ature and his loveliness came before the e[ye]s of his f[athe]r [a]nd his mother and all his relatives. They shall rejoic[e] over him more and more when they look upon him face to face and see his beauty and delightfulness.

**¶7** Just as in this simile, the wisdom that is present [in] the heart of the person is like the living child who is co[nceived] in the belly of his mother. And when he is taught and seals it in his heart, it becomes like the child who shall be born, and they see his beauty.

**¶8** So, in this way, the wisdom that the person proclaims, speak[ng] it from his heart, shall be advanced more and mor[e]. Its en-hancement and glory shall double from the time when the bea-uty and splendour of the saying will be displayed before the eyes [of t]hey who hear it, and it shall also advance for you [through the joy of] your hearing, and you are astonished at what you proclai[m].

**¶9** Once again, the wisdom is like this, while it is hidden in the heart of the person. [Bef]ore he has uttered it, it is just like [the blaze] of fire that is hidden in wood. An[d] that wood is [set aflame] by the blaze of the fire, but the garment of fir[e that exists in] the wood is not apparent. Indeed, you can see [many pieces of] ⟨p.207⟩ wood and they put them in a single house. It is impossible to [put li]ght to that house as long as the light within [the wood does not come forth] until the time when they are added to the fire, and the light [comes f]orth from them. It is possible for that entire house [to be] lit by the light of a single piece of wood.

**¶10** This is also the case with the wisdom that is in the heart of the person. [It] is like the fire that is hidden in the wood, as its light is not [d]isplayed. For its part, the wisdom is like this: its li[ght] is hidden and its glory is hidden in the heart; but when the person will proclaim it, its glory shall be displayed before the eyes and the ears of a multitude.

**¶13** For in this respect the wisdom is like this child about whom I have told you, the one who [was] born from the woman. Now, when he will be born, his father and mother and family circle shall rejoice over him. However, you find others grieving by reason of him, [s]ince they are strangers to him. These are not reckoned among his family. [They do n]ot rejoice over him, because he is not of their race.

**¶14** This is also [the case] with the wisdom. When it is proclaimed by the mouth of the teacher, these who are [a]kin to it shall receive it to them and rejoice in it; but [those] who are strangers to it neither rejoice in it [nor are they enlightened by it] nor receive it to them.

**¶15** Just like the light of [the fire, which I] proclaim[ed] to you, that shall come from the wood [and be apparent o]utside before the eyes of every one. ⟨p.208⟩ So, [wh]oever looks shall see the light that has [come fro]m the wood; but whoever is blind does not see the [fir]e.

**¶16** This is also the case with the wisdom, when it will be pro-claimed. The person, in whom is the [Mi]nd, of him is the wisdom. Whenever he may hear it, he shall receive it in to him; but the one who has no Mind in him is a stranger to it. He neither receives it in to him, nor shall he listen to [it].

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶1-3 [none]: Clean correspondential substrate: the teaching is by function (wisdom’s light/strength increases through utterance because it becomes shared and operative). Dialogue frame is coherent: disciple asks; 
> - ¶4-8 [none]: Clean correspondential simile with tight internal mapping: conception→hidden formation; birth→manifest operation; relatives rejoicing→multiple faculties/others participating. No sign of administrative
> - ¶9-10 [none]: Clean correspondential second simile (fire in wood/house). The chapter’s logic remains functional: latent potency becomes manifest illumination when released into act/communication. No administrative 
> - Between ¶10 and ¶13 (missing ¶11-12) [none]: Damaged/absent transmission seam rather than ideological insertion: the argument resumes smoothly at ¶13 with a back-reference (“in this respect”) to the child simile, suggesting intervening material 
> - ¶13-16 [none]: Clean correspondential closure on receptivity vs non-receptivity using kin/stranger and sight/blindness. Potential ‘ethnic/racial’ phrasing is not administrative; it functions as a correspondence for 

**Changes:** No changes made. All findings (1–5) carried a recommendation of none; there were no excisions to execute.

**Spiritual narrative:** When a person is admitted into interior discernment and receives living wisdom within the inmost love, and then fixes it in the formed teaching of the understanding, a deep gladness is born from conjunction. Yet the delight becomes greater when that inward wisdom is brought out into confession and use; for then it enlightens the whole person more fully, and what was inward becomes manifest, and by that manifestation truth is strengthened and power for life is taken up.

I perceive that what has been expressed is sound. I know that the wisdom I have received has been spread through my inner life and brought toward completion in my whole spirit. Yet it does not appear to me in its full brightness as something advanced and established unless it is brought out into actual expression and communicated to others. For when I bring it forth, I am giving it into the reception of another’s understanding. Would I have been moved toward it with such desire, and would my affection have been drawn to the wisdom I now express, if it had never come to me as something to be received and shared? Teach me why wisdom is more fully developed when it is expressed in act than when it is held inwardly in fixed form.

The question is rightly asked, and the instruction sought is great; from this comes the gladness that attends the uttering of wisdom. For wisdom is more fully alive in outward expression than in inward retention alone. The one who expresses it rejoices in it; and the one who receives it rejoices as well, is enlightened, and through that enlightenment is confirmed into enduring strength.

For it is as when a new life of good is first formed within the receptive love. It stirs within that affection and fills the interior state, so that the receiving love perceives and knows that a living beginning is present. There is a joy over that hidden formation until the time when it comes forth into open life. Then what was within is brought into freer activity, complete in its powers and without disorder, in a more open and spacious state of life than before; and it is then filled with the light of wisdom and speaks from living understanding, as one brought into a new degree of life.

At the first inward formation, the joy is not so great as when the formed life comes into open operation and is clearly seen at once in its quality and measure. Then love and joy are multiplied far beyond what they were while it was only held within.

For while it was being formed in concealment, its quality and its clear perception were withheld from the receiving love; but when it comes forth into open life, its quality is seen. Its measure and its loveliness come plainly before the perception of both love and understanding, and before all things in the person that are related and conjoined; and joy increases as it is more directly perceived in its true form and delight.

In the same way, wisdom present within the inmost love is like a living beginning of new life held within a receptive affection. When a person is taught and fixes it inwardly, it is like a life being prepared to come forth into view, so that its quality can be plainly perceived.

So wisdom, when it is brought forth into expression from within, is elevated more and more. Its increase and manifested excellence are multiplied from the time its inner beauty and brightness are presented to the understanding of those who receive it; and it advances also within the one who expresses it, because the bringing-forth opens reception anew, and the speaker then perceives with wonder what has been given to be expressed.

Again, wisdom is like this while it remains hidden within the person. Before it is brought forth, it is like inward love held in potency within the natural degree, present there as power but not yet visible as illumination. The natural degree can be disposed by that hidden ardor, yet the radiance within does not appear. Many such knowledges and forms can be gathered together within one outward life, and yet that outward life cannot be enlightened by them so long as the inward light within them is not released into clear understanding. But when they are joined to living love and thus opened, illumination goes forth from them; and a whole outward life can be enlightened from a single opened truth.

So it is with wisdom held within the inmost love: it is like inward love whose illumination is not yet displayed. In itself it is living, yet its light and excellence are hidden in the interiors; but when a person brings it forth into expression and communication, its excellence is made manifest before the perception and reception of many.

In this respect wisdom is like a new life of good brought into open operation: when it comes forth, everything in the person that is conjoined with it rejoices. Yet there are also states that are not conjoined which are distressed because of it, since they have no kinship with it; these are not numbered among its related affections and thoughts, and they do not rejoice because it is not of their quality.

So it is with wisdom when it is expressed by one who teaches: those who are in agreement with it in affection and understanding receive it and rejoice; but those who are not in agreement are strangers to it, and neither rejoice nor admit its light.

It is like illumination that becomes manifest in outward form for all who are able to perceive: whoever is in the capacity of understanding sees that illumination when it has gone forth into clarity, but whoever is in the deprivation of understanding does not perceive it.

So it is with wisdom when it is expressed: the one who has the inner faculty of understanding formed within receives it whenever it is heard, because wisdom belongs to that formed faculty; but the one who lacks that faculty is a stranger to it—such a one neither admits it nor truly attends to it.

---

## Chapter 85: Concerning the Cross of Light: [...] trample upon it.

**¶3** Now, when you walk on [your] way about some godly matter, as your commanding superior sends you, you do no sin therein. For you are not walking there about some lustful matter, nor concerning [love of gain] of advantage, wherein is sin; but you make your walkway due to the work of God, as you hasten [not] because of your own desire.

**¶4** Indeed, I, I tell you with a great voice: Every elect and righteous person who walks on a path due to the work of God, and though walking upon the earth and though treading with the soles of his feet on the Cross of Light, he does no sin therein. Rather, a crown and palm-branch is his entire path; because he walks not by his own desire, nor hastens about an acquisition and worthless things, as he tramples upon the earth and the Cross of Light.

**¶5** However, the other person who hastens on the path because of his own lust and [the lusts] of his body, as he [turns] his own about an acquisition and [worthless] things [everywhere]—a total sin is the walkway of that person, walking on the path of sin and error.

**¶6** [See,] behold, I have told you: Any one who, if his teacher will send him, goes on a path about some godly matter, a total victory is his path. Still, you also recounted this: “I trample upon the earth and the Cross of Light, I oppress with my [sole].” As you say: “Perhaps I have therein sin because of [the de]ed.”

**¶7** Again, do not be afraid even with this; because the living soul is like this. As a person, ⟨p.210⟩ if a disease will take hold of his limbs, and his heart [is sl]ow, his soul distressed, and he has pain in his disease. He seeks after a doctor to cure him, to make him strong of heart. And he cures, attends to, and gives him health [from] the disease that has risen up against him. So he thinks about this.

**¶8** [And] a wise person comes, knowing the formulas proper to a skilled doctor. If he shall achieve a healing of the one whom he [has entrusted] it, he recites over him the formulas that he knows, and he even tramples with his foot upon all his limbs! That person, who is ill, knows that this person is trampling upon him so that he might achieve a healing; rather, not doing this to him out of some enmity he had against him beforehand. Instead, all that he does, he does to him that he might make a healing of his body. And he expels [the] disease and the pain, trampling upon him to occasion this benefit. That person, who is ill, he can bear no anger nor hatred against this doctor, who tramples upon him. He can not hate him out of enmity, because he knows that he does this to him to his benefit.

**¶9** However, should someone come and trample upon him in enmity; he may hate, prevent and not permit him! For he knows that he tramples upon him in enmity; and as he strikes out of a wanton whim, he piles another illness upon his disease.

**¶12** Rather, the word that wounds is this: should a person utter a saying for the sake of the murder of a person, or the murder of animals, or for [the sake of] the murder of trees and the Cross of Light, the lying word and the wrathful and the bitter, or a word of envy and [hatred] only, or a word of accusation, which someone will spread against his brother; this is the [wor]d that wounds [the soul]. Should the person utter it, it befits [si]n; because [he has br]ought forth from it the demons [and the powers of Darkness] concerning this [word] the righteous person, or [and he binds himself] all of them, which I have proclaimed, in order that [he may bind] his word to the fetter; because anyone who would [bind] his word to the fetter. And again, he [binds] his coming forth, they will bind him in the place wherein he has bound his [soul].

**¶14** [He is li]ke this, a wise doctor who might turn back [the evil within] his disease. As a wound is in his body, he cuts [open] this person who is ill, in his wisdom, [and heals] he [so that he may uncover] this wound, and he cuts it. After these things [may draw out] and he places the soluble drugs upon it, so that [they will be able to diss]olve. He might incise it with the iron, and he casts it out ⟨p.212⟩ [f]rom him, with the soluble drugs. And the wound is purified [fr]om the fever. After these events, he shall place the soothing drugs [upon it] and it heals up. That person, who is ill, he shall give [thanks] but he shall not [hate him] he [shall not] generate anger in his heart against him; because he knows that he is making the aspect of his benefit and his healing. After the cut that he may make, and the cauterizing that he may do there, he shall even give rewards to the doctor! He gives his thanks and is a friend to him his whole life through.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶3-5 [none]: Clean correspondential teaching: innocence/culpability is determined by the function (intent/use) driving the walking, not by the mere external contact with earth/Cross-of-Light. The contrast (obedien
> - ¶6 [none]: Clean but rhetorically abrupt shift into quoted scruple (“I trample… I oppress…”). This is not an insertion of a different voice; it is a dialogical turn that motivates the ensuing medical analogy. Th
> - ¶7-9 [none]: Clean correspondential analogy block: ‘trampling’ can be therapeutic rather than hostile, depending on purpose. This directly resolves the scruple about trampling the Cross-of-Light by relocating the 
> - ¶12 [none]: Genuine difficulty from textual damage and syntactic corruption (likely lacunae and/or conflation), not a voice-insertion. The teaching remains correspondential (speech-as-act that wounds/binds), but 
> - ¶14 [none]: Clean correspondential continuation and closure: a second, more detailed medical analogy (incision/cautery/drugs) restates the same functional logic—painful intervention for healing—and lands the affe

**Changes:** No changes made. All findings recommended none (Findings 1–5). Paragraph 12 remains syntactically damaged per Finding 4, but no excision was recommended.

**Spiritual narrative:** When you advance in life under obedience to a higher leading, doing a holy use, no evil is imputed to you. For then your effort is not driven by craving, nor by self-serving advantage where sin is, but is ordered by the Divine work itself, and your urgency is not from selfhood.

I declare openly: every person who has been made receptive of good and truth, and who advances for the sake of Divine use, even while living and acting in the lowest and outermost degree, does not thereby incur sin. Rather, his whole progress is blessed and victorious, because he is not moved by self-will, nor carried by eagerness for possession and what is empty, even while he is engaged in outer works and in contact with what is holy in ultimates.

But the one who presses forward from his own craving and from the lusts of the natural life, turning his powers everywhere toward acquisition and vanity, makes his whole course evil; for he advances in sin and wandering, and his life is a pathway of falsity and error.

Understand, then: anyone who is sent by his instructor into a holy duty and goes to perform it has a path that is wholly victory. Yet you also turn your thought back on your outer actions and say within yourself that you seem to violate the lowest plane and what is holy there, as though you oppressed it by what you do, and you fear there may be sin in the deed.

Do not fear even this, because the living inner person is such that it can be burdened in its lower powers while it is being restored. It is as when disorder seizes a person’s faculties, the ruling love grows sluggish, the inner life is distressed, and pain is felt from that disorder. Then the person seeks one who has knowledge to heal and to strengthen what governs within; and the healer attends, restores, and brings soundness against the evil that had risen up, and the sufferer understands this purpose.

So also, one comes who is wise in the methods of genuine restoration. When he undertakes the cure of the one entrusted to him, he applies the truths he knows, and he even presses hard upon all the disordered powers. The one who suffers recognizes that this pressure is for the sake of healing, not from any prior hostility. Everything done is done so that the natural life may be restored to right order. Thus the evil and the pain are expelled by means that feel like oppression, because benefit is being brought about. Therefore the one being healed cannot rightly hold anger or hatred against the one who so presses upon his states, since he knows the action is for his good.

But if someone were to assault his states from hostility, he may rightly resist and refuse, because he perceives that the pressure is enmity; and what is done from reckless impulse adds a new evil to the evil already present.

Instead, what truly harms is a destructive utterance: when a person lets thought and intention go forth to destroy what has life—whether in another’s inner life, or in lower affections, or in perceptions—and even to violate what is holy in ultimates; when speech proceeds as falsity, as wrath, as bitterness, as envy and hatred, or as an accusation spread against a companion. This is the utterance that wounds the soul. If a person brings it forth, it is rightly sin, because by it he brings forth hellish influences and binds himself to them. For whoever binds his intention to such evil binds his own inner life; and when he comes forth into the other state, he is held where he had bound his soul.

It is like a wise healer who turns back the evil within the disorder. When corruption lies hidden in the natural life like an injury, he acts from discernment: he opens what is closed, exposes what is concealed, and separates it. Then he applies truths that can dissolve what has hardened, and he uses even sharper judgment that feels severe, casting out what is harmful by means that penetrate, until the fever of disorder is cleansed away. Afterward he applies gentler truths that soothe and restore conjunction, and the injured state is healed. The one restored gives thanks and does not form resentment against the one who corrected him, because he knows the work was for his benefit and recovery. Indeed, after the severe correction and the burning removal of evil, he rewards the healer with gratitude and remains conjoined in friendship thereafter.
