## Chapter 86: The Chapter of the Man who asks: Why [am] I sometimes at peace, and troubled at other times?

**¶2** Sometimes [I] am peaceful in my heart, as my consideration [is constructive], and all my doctrines are ordered and in place. I conduct [straightway in] my works, as I utter my words to all people with sweetness. I am peaceful of heart in the gladness of the Mind. ⟨p.214⟩ Even my body is carefree, and my soul [rejo]ices in wisdom and true knowledge.

**¶3** There are also times when I shall be troubled. My doctrines are confused. Gloom increases with them, and grief and anger and envy and lust. I am troubled, struggling with all my might that I would subdue them; not finding the strength of mind to subdue them at that hour nor day when they surged up in me. And all these evil teachings and wicked considerations come forth. They have tormented me. I do not comprehend, because there is no single shape in all these counsels that have entered me. Are they revealed to me, or indeed did they enter me from outside and have been shaken into me? I do not know if they have wakened within me alone, have rebelled inside me and set themselves up against me. I do not know about that. However, this only I understand: that I shall be very badly confused in all my doctrines. I shall do [error and disturbance] in my works, and you find my word is worthless in my mouth. I shall not find how [to be at peace in my heart], and I become a source of trouble and confusion for my brothers. Even my body rises up against me, and sickens for that entire day.

**¶6** Well do you ask about this lesson. Behold, I will explain it to you! ⟨p.215⟩ Understand this: The soul that assumes the body, when the Light Mind will come to it, shall be purified by the power of wisdom and obedience, and it is cleansed and made a new man. There is no trouble in (the soul), nor confusion nor disturbance.

**¶7** However, when a disturbance will arise for him and he will be troubled, this disturbance shall go into him in [many ways], first through his birth-signs and his difficult stars that [press upon him;] they turn over him and stir him and trouble him with lust and anger and depression and grief, as he wills. Also, as he wills, the powers of heaven shall trouble him through their roots, to which he is attached.

**¶8** Again, there are times you shall find the powers of heaven are peaceful and tranquil for him, and it comes to [another way; though] a difficult part comes into him by the nourishment that he has eaten, or else indeed in some [mixture of herbs] or in the water that they have drunk. Again, trouble and confusion and anger will increase in him, and lust multiplies upon him together with depression and grief; because of the nourishment of the bread he has eaten and the water he has drunk, which are full of bothersome parts, a vengeful counsel. They shall enter his body, [mixed in] with these foods, and they even become joined in with the wicked parts of the body and the sin that is in him; transferring the anger and the lust and the depression and the grief, these wicked thoughts of the body.

**¶10** There are times, however, if you shall find that the nourishment that comes into you is pure [and well blended], so it is greater in light and life while the waste is less in it, and the evil-doing is diminished from it. Even the atmosphere above is still, being purified through the [pure air and clear wind;] also the image is calmed from [every disturbance; and peace] increases in you through ⟨p.216⟩ those good parts of the food that come into you. And they find you at ease and tranquil, as you are well governed in your abodes because of the living part, which is greater on account of the light of these justified souls that are in it. The ones that have perfected their deeds. Their dues have ceased, while their soul is carefree [are sweet in your mouth;] they have been allied with these living souls that [are p]resent in you. Due to this you shall find them in you, calm in tranquillity, and they come from you without trouble; and you are found flourishing of body. Even your deeds are determined, being well constructed after their manner, and your doctrines are in order, as your words [because] your soul is carefree within you, ascending like a bird.

**¶11** Behold, I have explained to you the determination of this lesson for which you asked: Whence come these bad counsels and considerations into you, these vengeful thoughts that trouble; and also whence come into you these good counsels and sweet doctrines, when your Mind is calmed for you. I have told you that when [your bo]dy is troubled, you shall be troubled by the conduits of they below, the difficult parts that come into you by food and the act of eating. Again, when the conduits above are calmed from you, even your food that has come into you is clear and pure. Your heart shall be found ordered in its place, your counsels at peace from disturbance.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶2–3 [none]: Clean correspondential substrate: the chapter opens with an experiential alternation (peace vs turmoil) explained in terms of inner order/disorder and their functional effects on speech, works, commun
> - ¶6 [none]: Mostly correspondential, with a mild institutional phrasing that can sound like a later Mani-layer formula (“wisdom and obedience”) but it is integrated into the functional explanation (purification →
> - ¶7 [none]: Genuine difficulty: an astrological causal scheme (“birth-signs… difficult stars… powers of heaven… roots”) sits in the middle of an otherwise psychological/ethical correspondential teaching. The spir
> - ¶8 [none]: Clean correspondential development using physiological ingestion as functional causality (what enters/joins the body produces thoughts/affections). The reader’s note that it must be ‘generalized’ is a
> - ¶10 [annotate]: Possible seam-expansion within an otherwise coherent correspondential paragraph: the clause about “justified souls,” “dues,” and allied “living souls” introduces a mini-doctrinal aside that shifts fro
> - ¶11 [none]: Clean summary/conclusion that re-stitches the chapter’s causal explanation into a two-way schema (“conduits below” vs “conduits above”). The imagery is consistent with correspondential teaching (pathw

**Changes:** No changes made. The only critic action was an annotate recommendation within ¶10 (Finding 5), which per rules does not modify the chapter text. All other findings recommended none.

**Spiritual narrative:** There are states in which the will rests in quiet delight because the understanding is turned to what builds, and all the truths that have been learned stand in right order. Then the life of use proceeds evenly, and instruction goes out to others in a gentle form; the inner life is glad in enlightened intelligence, and even the outer life feels unburdened because the interior is in wisdom and genuine knowing.

Yet there are states in which unrest seizes a person. Truths lose their arrangement, and obscurity thickens together with a crowd of contrary affections—grief, anger, envy, and craving. Then a person strains to bring them into obedience, but in that moment no power of understanding is found to subject them, because they surge up with force. Perverse persuasions and evil intentions then come into action and torment the mind, so that nothing in them is coherent or distinctly formed. It becomes uncertain whether such disorder is being uncovered so it may be seen, or whether it has been admitted from without and driven inward; it is also uncertain whether it has awakened from one’s own selfhood and risen in rebellion against what is better. Only this is clearly perceived: the whole doctrine of life is thrown into confusion. Then uses are performed without right aim, teaching loses weight and truth in expression, and a person becomes an occasion of trouble and obscurity to those conjoined in charity. Even the outer life reacts against the inner disorder and falls into infirmity for that period.

This can be made plain. When the inner life is joined to the outer and an enlightened understanding comes, then by the power of wisdom in obedience the mind is purified and the life is cleansed and made new in form. In that renewal there is no unrest, no confusion, and no disturbance.

But when disturbance arises, unrest enters first through fixed tendencies and compulsions in the natural level—hereditary and acquired patterns that press and agitate the person into craving, anger, heaviness, and grief, according to the inclination then chosen. Moreover, insofar as that inclination invites it, influences from the spiritual environment trouble the person through the points of attachment by which the mind is conjoined to such influx.

There are also times when those higher influences are quiet and do not press, yet disorder still comes in through what is appropriated into the natural life and made one with it. Then irritation and confusion increase again, and anger and craving multiply with heaviness and grief, because what is admitted carries contentious and impure qualities. These are received into the outer life along with what is appropriated, and they join themselves to the evils and falsities already there; thus they transmit into the mind anger and craving, heaviness and grief, as impulses arising from the outer level.

But there are also times when what is appropriated is clean and rightly ordered, with more of what is living and enlightened in it and less of what is dead and harmful, and then evil influence is diminished. In such a state even the surrounding natural condition becomes still as the outer level is clarified, and the inner form is quieted from disturbance. Peace increases through the good that is made one with the person, and one is found at ease because the inner dwellings are better governed by the living element that then predominates through the illumination of those who are in a completed state of use and purification. In them the demand of guilt is ended, and the inner life is unburdened because it is conjoined with living goods and truths present with the person. Therefore these calm states are found within, and they pass through into act without causing trouble; the outer life flourishes, uses become determinate and well constructed in that same order, truths are restored to their places, teaching proceeds rightly, and the inner life is free, rising into higher thought.

This is the settling of the matter: whence come the bitter intentions and disorderly reasonings that trouble, and whence come good intentions and gentle ordered truths when the understanding is quiet. When the outer level is agitated, trouble is induced through lower avenues by impure elements that are admitted and appropriated; but when the higher avenues are quiet, what is appropriated is also clear and pure, and then the will is found set in right order and the counsels of the mind rest in peace without disturbance.

---

## Chapter 87: Concerning the Alms, that [ ... ] life in the Church.

**¶5** What does the assembly of catechu[me]ns resemble? It is like good earth [made ready] in which the gardener shall plant a good tree. [Beho]ld, [th]at good tree transforms the power and the [quality] of all earth, and it produces good fruit. [Th]at [gardener] sets at ease the tree of life, and he r[ests himself].

**¶7** Likewise this [liv]ing sou[l] that today is set in mixture; for it wishes to [as]cend and go to the house of its people, but it kn[ow[s a]]nd understands that it has no open door in all the powers of heaven and earth. For they are its oppression that [binds] it everywhere. Indeed, it has no open door except the sun and the moon themselves, the light-givers of the heavens [whereby] they become the place of rest for it. And they become a door that opens for it [in] the coming forth, and (the soul) comes out through them to the country of the household of i[ts] people.

**¶8** Again, further in, also the light-givers of the heavens have no place of rest amongst all the powers of heaven; except for the lan[d] of light, that has indeed been theirs for ever.

**¶9** So, thus the living soul is purified of all afflictions; and they provide a rest for it, and open a door that opens to the house [of] its people.

**¶10** Also, it is the case that the land of lig[ht] will become the final receiver for the light-givers of [the] heavens, and they will rest themselves therein and rule in it [for] ever.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶5 [none]: Clean correspondential simile: catechumens ↔ prepared earth; gardener ↔ planter; tree ↔ implanted life; fruit ↔ works. No narrative break; the spiritual reading can derive meaning by function (prepara
> - ¶7 [none]: Clean correspondential liberation sequence expressed in cosmological imagery. While “sun and moon” could invite later administrative/systematic cosmology, here they function as the only “open door” an
> - ¶8 [none]: Clean continuation, not an overlay: it tightens the ‘rest’ motif by relocating it from intermediate powers to the ultimate ‘land of light.’ Still correspondential (rest-place logic), not administrativ
> - ¶9 [none]: Clean summarizing consequence: purification → rest provided → door opened. This is the expected resolution of ¶7–8 and contains no foreign teaching mode.
> - ¶10 [none]: Minor mixed-voice coloration: “rule … for ever” introduces governance language, but it is embedded as a concluding stability claim about the final resting-place of the light-givers, not a jurisdiction

**Changes:** No changes made. The critic issued no excise recommendations for ¶5, ¶7, ¶8, ¶9, or ¶10 (Findings 1–5: recommendation ‘none’).

**Spiritual narrative:** A state of instruction and preparation is like a receptive outward mind made ready for implantation. Into such a receptivity a ministering Divine influence sets a living perception of good. Once implanted, that perception reorganizes the whole strength and quality of the external life, and from that reordering it brings forth deeds that are genuinely good. When this living good has been established in tranquility, the inmost life is set in peace, and the Divine operation rests in the accomplished use.

In the same way, a living consciousness that is now held in confused conjunction with lower things desires to be raised into the communion where its own goods and truths belong; yet it perceives that no passage stands open anywhere in the full range of its faculties, because disordered powers press upon it and constrain it on every side. No opening is found except through the paired illuminations that give discernment and make understanding shine, and in that illumination it is granted a quiet dwelling. Through this ordered light a passage is formed at the moment of emergence, and by it the consciousness is led out of confusion into its own homeland of kindred life.

Yet even these illuminations find no settled repose within the intermediate powers themselves, but only in the inmost state of pure enlightenment, which has been their own eternally.

So the living consciousness is cleansed from every distress born of disorder; interior quiet is provided; and a genuine entrance is opened into the communion of its own life.

And that inmost state of pure enlightenment becomes the final recipient of all true illumination, where it rests and exercises lasting governance, because it remains conjoined to its origin in unending order.

---

## Chapter 88: Concerning the Catechumen who found fault with the Elect: why he is angry.

**¶5** This is also the case with the elect. They are gods as they stand firm in the image of the gods. The divinity that is planted in them came to them from the heights and [ab]elt in them.

**¶6** Further, when they will be troubled and angry and resentful, [they] curse each other. However, do not you be troubled in your heart. You know, once and for all, they are established in a body that is not their own, hating the Flesh of Sin that makes the other dwell in a foreign land. Due to this they shall be angry, and say and utter hard words to each others' faces.

**¶7** For you have known the mystery of the two essences, you have understood that what is good and what is evil dwell in every person. You have also understood that the saints bear a great burden on their shoulders. For you yourself have understood that they carry a great burden, they are established in the body that is not their own.

**¶8** Your hands are always free to beat the Cross of Light.

**¶11** For when quarrelling will arise among the saints [it is] because the old man too dwells in their body; and due to this they shall take offence [and fall into mutual opposition,] they {dispute} with one another.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶5-¶7 [none]: Clean correspondential substrate: a functional psychological-spiritual teaching about mixed states in the elect/reformed (divinity implanted from above; conflict arising from the ‘foreign’ body; two e
> - ¶8 [none]: Genuine difficulty (compressed symbol) rather than an insertion: the phrase is abrupt but still fits the chapter’s functional logic as a practical pivot from diagnosis (why quarrels happen) to respons
> - ¶11 [none]: Clean continuation and closure: it restates the causal mechanism of quarrels (the ‘old man’ remaining in the body) in the same correspondential mode as ¶6-¶7. No sign of administrative overlay or seam

**Changes:** No changes made. The critic recommended none for ¶5–¶7 (Finding 1), ¶8 (Finding 2), and ¶11 (Finding 3), and identified 0 excisions and 0 annotations.

**Spiritual narrative:** So it is with those who are being reformed. They are divine in measure as they remain steadfast in a living reception of what is Divine. The Divine that has been implanted in them flows down from higher degrees and abides within their interiors.

Yet when their lower mind is stirred into unrest, and the will is heated by injured self-regard, they turn against one another and send forth condemnations from falsity. Do not let this disturb your inner peace. You know conclusively that they are held, for a time, in an outward form of life that is not yet wholly made their own, and they feel aversion toward the inherited and acquired tendency to evil that drives the mind to serve what is alien to heaven. From this conflict they are provoked, and the bitterness that lies in the natural self breaks out into harsh speech face to face.

For you have been admitted into the arcanum of the two opposing principles within the human mind, and you understand that both the inclination toward good and the inclination toward evil are present in every person. You also understand that those who are being made holy sustain a heavy spiritual labor within their operative powers, because they endure the weight of this combat while they are still being held in an external life not yet fully conjoined to their inward renewal.

Your powers of action remain at liberty to resist and subdue the assault upon saving truth and Divine love.

For when contention rises among those who are in the good of religion, it is because the former self still lives in their external degree; and from this they take offence and fall into mutual opposition, and so they enter into disputes with one another.

---

## Chapter 89: The Chapter of the Nazorean who questions the Teacher

**¶6** “The judge is not a man of wickedness, but rather the work counteracts wickedness, limiting the evil. Nevertheless, what you have recounted is that the judge shall go and punish, kill and slay. He acts like this that he might annul the evil and impede the wickedness in the person; and lay it upon the head of the sinner.

**¶11** “Nevertheless, God has not done anything that is ev[il to] them, rather they to themselves. It is their own deeds that shall con- demn them and cast them in the gehenna of burning.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶6 [none]: Coherent correspondential teaching: “judge/punish” language is explained as the function of order limiting evil, not as a jurisdictional or mythic administration. No seam, no list, no named powers. Th
> - ¶11 [none]: Coherent continuation in the same correspondential voice: the causal account (“they do it to themselves”) keeps the narrative line and explains ‘gehenna’ as the experiential outcome of chosen deeds. N
> - ¶6–¶11 [none]: No detectable voice-boundary seam or Mani-style administrative expansion. The chapter is very short (likely excerpted), but what survives is internally consistent and reads like a single teaching unit

**Changes:** No changes made. Finding 1 (¶6) recommended none; Finding 2 (¶11) recommended none; Finding 3 (¶6–¶11) found no seam or expansion and recommended none.

**Spiritual narrative:** Discernment that proceeds from the Divine is not wickedness; it is the effect of order resisting wickedness, restraining it, and setting bounds to its action. Yet to the outward view that same effect is spoken of as if it goes forth to punish and destroy, because it removes what is contrary to good and truth, brings its activity to an end, and so annuls evil and blocks its power within the person, turning it back upon its own source and fixing it as the person’s own guilt.

Still, the Divine does nothing evil to anyone; they do it to themselves. Their own chosen acts condemn them, and those acts carry them into the tormenting intensity of evil love and its consuming unrest.

---

## Chapter 90: Concerning the Fifteen Paths; and whether the Catechumen would have avoided causing his Wealth to go on the Three Paths to Gehenna

**¶2** For we have heard thus from you: ‘Fifteen paths are drawn out in the zone. Four paths are pure and belong [to the] light, leading up to life. Eight other paths are [mixed], leading above from that place. The light shall go [u]p and become free through them; be purified and go in [to the] ships. However, the waste is separated and thrown [dow]n to transmigr[a]tion and the other three paths of [waste are] discharged to the gehennas’.

**¶3** You have explained [that the three] paths that belong to waste, the ones that are drawn to the gehennas, [a]re drawn from the fleshes. O[n]e of them is the appetite for [lawl]essness, that of people and of all flesh. The second is the [destruction of] all bodies, which is slaughter, [with] which all flesh is consumed. The third is the damaging action, which [wounds] the Cross of Light and every body; together with the rest of the error and the blasphemy that wounds the gods.

**¶4** Th[ese th]ree paths have you taught us, that they lead to the gehennas. And you have also [told] us that every person shall follow after hi[s] de[e]ds, whether to life or indeed to death.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶2–¶4 [none]: No narrative break or voice-switch. The chapter is a compact correspondential teaching about functional ‘paths’ (ways of operation) and their outcomes (ascent/purification vs separation/descent), then
> - ¶2 [none]: Clean substrate correspondential structure with numeration serving the teaching (4 pure / 8 mixed / 3 waste) rather than a taxonomic ‘inventory’ of officials. The numeration organizes outcomes and pur
> - ¶3 [none]: Clean correspondential triad: three hell-leading ‘paths’ are defined by characteristic operations (lawlessness, destruction/slaughter, injuring/profaning). The presence of distinctively Manichaean tec
> - ¶4 [none]: Stable conclusion that matches the chapter’s functional-moral arc; not a seam-expansion. It generalizes the preceding process into a universal law of outcome according to deeds, which is a typical cor

**Changes:** No changes made. All findings (1–4) recommended none; there were no excisions or annotations to apply.

**Spiritual narrative:** Within the outermost spiritual domain a complete arrangement of ways of operation is established. A sufficient total is opened there: some ways are wholly from genuine good and truth and therefore lead by orderly ascent into conjunction with living good; others are compound ways in which truth is mingled with what is not truth, and by these the understanding can be raised out of bondage, made free, and purified so far as it submits to higher influx, until it is received into the organized forms of doctrine and use where truths can serve the purposes of heaven. But whatever in that ascent is mere defilement is separated out; it is cast downward into the repetitive bondage of falsity and evil, and the remaining ways that are wholly defiled are driven into the hells.

You have further explained that the ways which are wholly defiled, and therefore plunge into the hells, arise from the lowest proprial loves that cling to corporeal life. One is the delight of casting off all restraint of truth and good, a universal craving that wants to act from self without law and without heaven. The second is the total destruction of what has form for reception, a violent hatred that consumes all good and truth so that nothing of spiritual life remains. The third is the will to injure and profane what is Divine in the truth that saves, and thus to assault every form of life in the human, together with the rest of the turning away, the falsity, and the denial that strikes against what is holy.

These are the ways you have taught that lead into the hells; and you have also taught that each person is carried by the actual quality of his or her works into the state that agrees with them, whether into the life of heaven or into spiritual death.

---

## Chapter 91: Also concerning the Catechumen; shall he be saved in a single Body?

**¶3** The sign of that perfect catechumen is this: You shall find the woman in the house with him, and beside him she is like these strangers. His house, in his reckoning, shall be like these lodging houses. He says: I am living in a house for rent by days and months. His brothers and his relatives shall be, in his reckoning, necessary as foreign people who take up with him, while travelling on the road with him. He [knows that, after a time,] they will separate from him, and each one [of them] may [take] the gold and the silver and the utensils of the [same] house. To him, they shall be like borrowed vessels; [he] takes them, is served by them, and afterwards gives them to their owner. He does not set his trust on them, nor his treasure.

**¶8** When, however, they come forth from their body, they travel on their way and pass by in the place above, and go in to the life. They shall be purified in the heavens, and they are plucked just as a fruit that ripens is plucked from the tree. In just the same way, this alms-offering that passes over to the elect is given likeness in many forms. It is purified and goes in to the land of the living. Similarly, the souls of the catechumens who shall not enter (another) body resemble (the alms).

**¶13** Every person who has received the hope and the faith and has separated the light from the darkness, and has perceived the mysteries of the living soul, he has received the right hand of peace from the Light Mind

**¶20** As for the rest, he shall be questioned about a single part; and receive blows for (those sins) and retribution. Afterwards he is purified, whether indeed above or below. He shall be purified according to the worth of his deeds, and cleansed and washed and adorned. Afterwards, he is sculpted a light image; and [he glides]es up and reaches the land of rest, so that the place where his heart is, his treasure also will be there.

**¶24** the first light, the one that brings understanding through the Mind and [the] riches of its knowledge.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶3 [none]: Clean correspondential substrate: a coherent functional teaching by enacted metaphors (house-rental, travelers, borrowed vessels) describing detachment from externals. The spiritual reading did not st
> - ¶8 [none]: Clean correspondential continuation: post-mortem process is taught by functional analogies (travel, purification, fruit ripening, alms transference). No narrative seam; the catechumen theme remains ac
> - ¶13 [none]: Potential micro-seam but not disruptive: a brief gnomic summary (‘everyone who… has received…’) that condenses the prior process into a criterion and gift. It remains correspondential (states of disce
> - ¶20 [none]: Clean correspondential eschatological mechanics: examination → blows/retribution → purification above/below → cleansing/adornment → ‘sculpted’ light image → ascent/rest. This is functional transformat
> - ¶24 [none]: Likely closing colophon-like doxological tag, but still compatible with the substrate: it names the source principle (‘first light’/Mind/knowledge) rather than introducing administrative machinery. Re

**Changes:** No changes were made. All critic recommendations were “none” (Findings 1–5), with no excisions or annotations required.

**Spiritual narrative:** The mark of one fully being introduced into a new life is this: within the person there is truth conjoined with the good it is meant to serve, yet it is held as though not one’s own possession. The whole outward condition is regarded as a temporary arrangement granted for a time, not as a permanent dwelling. All associations of kinship and companionship are valued only so far as they are necessary for the present progress, like passing connections permitted along the way, known to be loosened afterward. The knowledges, skills, and external supports used for a season are understood to be taken back into their proper order when their use is finished. Therefore such a person treats these things as capacities entrusted for service: receiving them, being helped by them for a time, and then yielding them back without fastening the heart on them. 

When such people are released from the outermost life, they continue according to the ruling love they have acquired, are led onward through an intermediate state, and are introduced into a more interior life. There they undergo purification in higher degrees: whatever is incompatible with heaven is removed, and whatever has come to maturity is taken up into the living order. In the same way, the good of charity that has proceeded from genuine love is received by those who can bear it, is freed from what clung to it from lower motives, is diversified into many forms of use, and is admitted into the life that endures. So too, the inner life of those being formed who do not return into another outward state is received, purified, and elevated according to the same pattern.

Everyone who has accepted inward expectation and trust, who has truly distinguished what enlightens from what obscures, and who has been granted perception of the secrets by which the inner person lives, has also received conjunction with peace from Divine Wisdom.

What remains that is still disordered is brought into examination; there is painful correction and repayment according to the quality of the evils. Afterward comes purification, in a higher or lower state as is required, according to the worth of the life actually lived. Thus the person is cleansed, renewed, and invested with what is fitting; then the person is brought into a formed spiritual identity receptive of light, and is raised into a state of rest, so that the inmost love becomes the abiding possession.

All of this is from the first proceeding illumination: the primary light that grants understanding through wisdom, together with the fullness of its interior knowledge.

---

## Chapter 92: The Apostle is asked: Why when you drew every thing in the Picture (- Book), did you not draw the Purification of the Catechumens who shall be cleansed by Transmigration?

## Chapter 93: A Catechumen asked the Apostle: When I would give an Offering to the Saints, shall I inflict a Wound on the Alms?

**¶4** For this matter is like to two people, if a personal enemy and an opponent of theirs will hit one first and make a sore and a wound on his body. Afterwards he smites him again and [presses him] and he hits over his blows and [renews] blows that have made sores on him and [the] ⟨p.237⟩ strikes him these two times [the] accuses his enemy who has struck him. And this judge questions him to his face about the [and the injured one] of the sores that he has left as marks on his body. And they [quality] on him as if he had split his head. And he is led to judgement and sentence, and is punished before the judge of the world. He makes retribution for a sore in the sore place, and adds to him the wound that he had left on this person who had not yet sinned against him. He shall not omit the retaliation of the opponent, as he is judged [in this way] according to enmity.

**¶5** After the end, this enemy is condemned for the retaliation [and for the] sin against him. Afterwards, the other one is sick [again] the one whom this enemy has struck, and a wound comes out on him [from the wound] and he becomes a sick person by cause of his illness, and he [seeks help] He calls the doctor and shows him his wound. However, that doctor has no [enmity] nor [retaliation] and he takes the knife to him and cuts this wound. Nevertheless, when he cuts it the blood shall pour out of his wound. That person shall be pained, but he accepts those and these things. This doctor has not retaliated nor was harm imposed on him; because he did this thing to him for healing his wound. All that he did to him was done for the good; and not directly for evil.

**¶6** After the completion, he cuts this wound and lets blood from it and heals it. He shall even make [him] gifts and compliments. That illness and the healing become a pride for this doctor, and he is given honour.

**¶7** This is also how this matter of [healing] before this living soul. [It is] At the time ⟨p.238⟩ [when truth first approaches it,] it [seems as though] you have struck [it,] it is well and [afterwards] ease shall exist for it. [and] A glory comes about for this [rest and] healing [thereafter] it exists [work of] it is painful [for the moment, though]

**¶8** Also, [this] is the other person [who] struck the comrade of the [enemy] sick with his [living soul, being] he struck in [cruelty;] he being cruel according to [envy] living soul [and he struck the] upon it [he laid hands] that [the] accusation of [enmity]

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶4–¶6 [none]: Coherent correspondential substrate: the teaching arises from the functional difference between (a) an enemy’s repeated assault that leaves a mark and triggers retributive judgement, and (b) a doctor’
> - ¶7 [none]: Genuine difficulty from damaged/fragmentary transmission, not a secondary administrative insertion. The paragraph is the expected applicative turn (“So it is…”) but key clauses are broken, forcing the
> - ¶8 [none]: Genuine difficulty from corruption/fragmentation, yielding a clumsy contrastive coda. The intended contrast (cruel/envious assault vs healing correction) fits the chapter’s functional logic, but the s

**Changes:** No changes made. All findings (1–3) carried recommendation “none,” and no excision targets were identified.

**Spiritual narrative:** This matter may be grasped by seeing the difference between harm that comes from hostility and correction that comes from care for restoration. When a will set against good first assaults another’s life and damages what should have remained whole, and then returns to press the harm further—renewing it and fixing it more deeply—what has been injured remains as a lasting trace. When this is brought before interior discernment, the one who has been harmed appeals to what judges justly within. Then that judging faculty examines the aggressor directly and searches out the quality of the damage that remains as a mark in the life, exposing the violence as though the very power of understanding had been split. The case is carried into spiritual judgement; a sentence is formed, and the evil is permitted to return into its own kind, so that the one who willed injury undergoes the measure he imposed. Because the assault was begun against one who had not first acted from such hostility, the condemnation is intensified according to the injustice of that beginning; and the opposition is not excused, because judgement follows the quality of enmity itself.

When this process has run its course, that hostile will is condemned for acting from retaliation and for setting itself in sin against another. Yet afterward the one who was harmed can still fall into a state of weakness, as the earlier damage opens into an active disorder in the life. In distress he turns to restoring wisdom and lays bare the disorder. That restoring wisdom holds no hatred and seeks no repayment; it applies keen truth to the corrupted state and makes a saving separation. In that work, interior life is released with force, and the person feels pain, yet accepts it because the end is healing. No injustice is in it, because the entire act is governed by good and not by evil as its direct aim.

When the work is completed, what was disordered is opened, what must be removed is let out, and the life is made sound. Gratitude and acknowledgement then follow, and the former distress together with the restoration becomes a testimony to the healer’s use, so that honour is given.

So it is with this whole matter in the presence of the living principle within a person: when truth first touches a disordered state it can feel like injury, yet it is good when it proceeds from the intention to set in order, and afterward it brings rest. From such restoration there arises glory for the healing work, even though the moment of correction is painful.

But there is also the opposite kind of action: one who attacks a companion in shared life from cruelty and envy of what is living, laying violent hands on the other’s state. Over such a will the accusation stands, because its impulse is enmity rather than the good of restoration.

---

## Chapter 94: Concerning the Purification of these Four Elements [that have been place]d in the Flesh.

**¶2** The four elements [are what is] purified. From the beginning they are holy ones, living ones, and enlighteners. However, when they undertood [the assault of the enemy] in that first struggle, the waste was [mixed] in with them, and the Darkness of the enemy was mixed with them. And when they were come into the body, stripping off these polluted garments, they set them in the flesh. Their pollutions, which they have stripped off, shall be displayed in a [lower place] and become a danger to the body.

**¶3** The discharge of this pollution, that [the fir]e shall disgorge and place in the body, is [blo]od. The discharge of the blood is anger. The discharge of anger is the humours.

**¶4** ⟨p.240⟩
Also, the emissions that the waters discharge from them are lust. The discharge of lust is bitterness. The emission of bitterness is f[e]ver.

**¶5** The emission that light shall disgorge is flesh. The emission of flesh is gloom. [The] emission of gloom is obstinacy.

**¶6** The emissions that the tree shall disgorge, they are the voices of the winds of shame. The emission of winds of shame [is hardness of heart]

**¶7** The emission of the [tear] is the [completion] of these twelve emissions.

**¶8** [Afterwards] these four elements shall [be cleansed] what are gathered in are [f]ound by the management of this soul food [that] enters the body. When they enter the body, they are cleansed and purified and established [i]n their living image, which is the new man. They shall live [according to their order] and receive the Light Mind and be purified in their image; and they come forth, being cleansed and holy. They attain their first rest.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶2 [none]: Coherent correspondential substrate: the chapter sets up a functional purification narrative (holy elements → mixture with darkness → stripping garments → deposits in flesh/lower place) that grounds t
> - ¶3-5 [none]: Coherent correspondential chains, but with mild semantic strain caused by translation/lexical ambiguity rather than insertion. The ‘discharge/emission’ staircase (X disgorges Y → Y discharges Z → Z di
> - ¶6-7 [none]: Genuine difficulty (compressed or slightly damaged correspondential symbolism), not an administrative insertion. The chain’s first member (“tree… voices of the winds of shame”) is unusually metaphoric
> - ¶8 [none]: Coherent correspondential resolution with a rhetorical shift (from corrupt emissions to purification/rest) that remains in the same functional teaching mode; no administrative overlay. The shift is ex

**Changes:** No changes made. All findings (1–4) recommend none; ¶6–7 are noted as damaged/compressed but not to be excised.

**Spiritual narrative:** A complete sufficiency of fundamental principles in a person is what undergoes purification. In the first state these principles are set apart for the Divine, filled with living power, and able to illumine. Yet when they first perceived the assault of evil in the initial conflict, what was useless and rejected became mingled into them, and an opposing falsity from evil was conjoined with them. When these principles were brought down into the outer life, they cast off the defiled outward forms that had clung to them and pressed those defilements into the merely natural self. The impurities that were cast off are made manifest in a lower plane and become a hazard to the outer life.

From the love-principle, when it is defiled, there comes an outflow forced out into the outer life: falsified truth made vivid in the person. From that falsified truth there breaks forth contentious zeal, and from contentious zeal there proceeds a spread of disordered states in the natural mind.

Likewise, from the truth-principle, when it is defiled, there are outflows it expels from itself: unchaste craving. From unchaste craving there proceeds aversion, and from that aversion there arises a consuming agitation of the natural life.

From the wisdom-principle, when it is defiled, there is an outflow it expels: a merely external life severed from inner spirit. From that external life there proceeds obscurity of understanding, and from that obscurity there arises a fixed resistance against reformation.

From perverted perception there are outflows it expels: persuasive impulses that proclaim disgrace and self-condemnation. From those persuasive impulses there follows a final hardening of the will against tenderness and change.

And from grief in the lower self there comes forth the last outflow, which completes the full series of corrupt outgoings.

After this, these fundamental principles are again taken up for purification. What has been gathered within the person is found and set in order through the guidance of inward nourishment that enters the outward life. When these principles enter the outer life in right order, they are cleansed and fixed in their living form, which is the regenerated person. Then they live according to that order, receive an enlightened understanding from the Divine, and are purified into that likeness; so they come forth cleansed and set apart, and in this they reach their first peace.

---

## Chapter 95: The Apostle asks his Disciples: What is Cloud?

**¶2** Happen you know that it shall be released from five places and ascend over this great earth and [in the sea and in the rivers] and it is revealed and seen in the atmosphere between.

**¶3** So, the cloud shall ascend from fire to the heavens towards the likeness of the Virgin of Light that she shall display to it. Its sign is the flashes that occur with lightning storms [and]ts exchange [in the cloud]. And they shall be purified by her towards [the place of the Virgin of Light] the light that she shall reveal [to it]. The rulers shall be released by the lightning storms and they are freed and sent.

**¶4** However, [these] angels shall immediately be summoned to them, so that they will seize them, because they know that they shall never do good. Every place they will touch they shall make dead and a loss. Whenever the angels will make towards them and come beside [them], they shall flee in their constraint before them. They go into whatever they will meet upon and assume it, like a robber being abandoned and fleeing before one stronger than him, as he runs after him to arrest him.

**¶5** Now, those rulers that shall be freed from these clouds and assume whatever they will meet upon [and enter] into the [trees] that they did not [find the way,] will seize this thing, whether indeed a tree to which they have found the way, or animals, or people. Simply, every place that they will assume they shall burn and destroy by the fire of their body. Namely, the rulers, [the] children of fire, they that shall be stripped of the fire.

**¶6** In contrast, [the] cloud that shall be raised up from water, and comes above towards the Virgin of Light; its sign is the thunder storms and the booms that shall come in the atmosphere in various forms and are heard. [And] Again, there are times if the rulers that belong to them, which exist in the cloud, shall on occasion be constrained [in a] water [clou]d. And they flee from it and abandon it.

**¶7** Now, [they] too are so, in that every place that they will [reach], they shall make frost and hail and snow. [They rui]n the seed-corn and the fruits and the plants; and they [waste] every place [that] they will reach.

**¶8** Again, [ang]els shall be summoned to them who seize them. Still, while the angels [ru]sh after them in order to seize them, they shall make this destruction.

**¶9** Also, [the cl]oud that will be raised up from wind ascends to the atmosphere between, towards the likeness of the Virgin that she shall display to it. Its sign is [the s]torm wind that shall blow with bitterness.

**¶10** So, in those winds and tempests shall the lives be purified. Again, the rulers will be stripped of the cloud of wind and they descend from it, safe in their image. All the rivers and the seas that they will reach and arrive at, they shall make waves in them and raise [billows] and they [overturn] the ships. They wreak destruction [in the seas] and they seize and bring them [downwards].

**¶11** Also the cloud, the one of light, immediately they will be stripped of it [before] the Virgin. She shall display its sign too [as well]. And its sign is this, in that you shall find it sailing in stillness and calm. The lives in it shall be cleansed in stillness and calm. Again, the rulers that will be stripped of it [in the atmosphere] that will come forth [are those] they shall put [from it,] and they take [upon] there [whatever they will meet upon;] the angels seize them.

**¶12** Again, the cloud that shall be raised up from the mingled air comes above towards [the image] of the Virgin. Its sign is this, in that [the lives] are purified in it in stillness and [repose. Again,] that will be stripped of that cloud and come from it; every place that they will find [upon it,] they shall make it [dead] one of [another] until [the angels come upon them] they are seized and taken to their midst.

**¶13** Their strippings, that they shall leave behind them in various places they will have reached, whether the produce of the trees or the offspring of the fleshes, they shall be taken midst [their place, and they] wither away. They shall inflict great dangers, but again those rulers also the angels shall send to their midst. They bring the light from them and cast the demons down to these seven outer ditches of the great sea; which is the vessel of the waters that are outside the world.

**¶15** Those ditches and the [descents continue] until they reach the earths below [the] feet of the Porter.

They beseech them [to let them go,] they that are given the command. However, [t]hose [rul]ers shall be freed from them alone and assume [tree] and [the fle]sh. They are consumed by this offspring [of] tree and that of flesh. The angels shall set a [guard] over them, that they might guard them until the hour when [those r]ulers will be stripped of tree and [flesh; a]nd they go up above. They shall be gathered [together] above, and they are poured out [again] in their first for[ms and their first im]ages in which they first were. And they are cast to the outer prison by the angels, when the command is given to them through the power of the Virgin of Light. She has authority over the entire zone, and cleanses the life that is in it.

**Changes:** No changes made. The critic’s findings (1–11) recommended ‘none’ throughout, with only localized damage/opacity noted (¶3, ¶12, ¶15) and mild administrative coloration in ¶16 judged integral; therefore no excisions were warranted.

**Spiritual narrative:** Know that disordered governing falsities, long held bound within a sufficient range of outermost states, will be loosened, and their operation will rise and spread through the whole natural degree; and this ascent will be made manifest within the intermediate region where inner and outer meet.

Then an obscuring state will be lifted by the arousal of perverted love toward higher perception, and it will be brought into the presence of manifested divine wisdom in the form of pure truth. The mark of it is sudden outward eruptions that attend conflicts of falsity, together with reciprocal disturbances within that state. By that divine wisdom whatever can be amended there is purified insofar as it can be turned toward the truth then disclosed; and the governing falsities that had ruled within it are loosened by those conflicts, driven out, and dispatched.

Yet protective powers are at once directed to restrain them, because their quality is known: they cannot will good. Wherever their influence touches, they induce spiritual death and loss. When the protective powers turn toward them and draw near, those falsities retreat under inner compulsion. They insinuate themselves into whatever disposition they encounter and take it on, as evil hides in a form while fleeing the stronger good that pursues to bring it under order.

Thus the governing falsities, once expelled from those obscuring states, enter into whatever they can seize in the outer life—into some structure of perception, or into affections, or into human states. Wherever they take possession, they consume and devastate by the ardor of their own craving; for they are powers of self-love, offspring of perverted love, deprived of heavenly love.

But an obscuring state arising from falsified understanding is also lifted and brought upward toward that same manifestation of divine wisdom. Its mark is the rumbling and recurring outbursts that are heard in the intermediate region in many variations. There are times when the governing falsities proper to that state are constrained within such falsified understanding; then they recoil from it and abandon it.

Even so, wherever they extend, they induce hardening and chilling in the outer life. They ruin interior truths in potency, and the works that would proceed from them, and the growing forms of spiritual life; they waste whatever region they reach.

Again protective powers are directed to restrain them; yet while those protective powers press after them to seize them, these falsities still pour out their devastation.

There is also an obscuring state arising from unstable persuasion, which rises into the intermediate region toward that same displayed form of wisdom. Its mark is a vehement impetus of persuasion that drives with bitterness.

Within such violent persuasions the lives held there are purified by being forced out of falsity toward order. Again the governing falsities are stripped of that persuasive covering and descend from it secure in their own character. Wherever they reach the flowing truths of intelligence and the general knowledges of the outer mind, they agitate them into turmoil and raise commotions; they overturn what carries the mind in outward uses and doctrines. They wreak devastation and seize and drag down what they can.

There is also an obscuring state that appears as enlightenment; it too is immediately taken away in the presence of divine wisdom, which shows its mark. Its mark is that it presents itself as tranquil and undisturbing. The lives within it are cleansed through quiet order. Again the governing falsities deprived of that seeming enlightenment come forth; they attempt to appropriate whatever they can, but there protective powers seize them.

Again there is an obscuring state from mixed and confused influx, which also rises toward the displayed form of wisdom. Its mark is that purification occurs within it through quiet and repose; yet the governing falsities stripped of that confusion and coming forth profane and damage whatever they find in the outer life in one way after another, until they are seized and brought into custody.

The coverings they leave behind in the regions they have invaded—whether the works that had proceeded from perverted perception or the derivative formations of corrupted life—are gathered back into the midst of their own domain and wither away. They bring severe spiritual dangers; yet those governing falsities also protective powers send into their midst. The light is drawn out of them, and the hellish forces are cast down into a full series of outermost prisons within the general knowledges that lie outside the ordered mind, which serve as containing forms for falsities beyond the spiritual world.

These prisons and their descents continue until they reach the lowest natural ultimates beneath the final boundary of restraint.

Hear further: at times protective powers are sent to restrain the governing falsities. Whenever the governing falsities are pursued, they plead to avert the restraint, for the protective powers hasten after them by command. Yet those governing falsities are freed only for a time and then take on borrowed external forms of perverted perception and corrupted life. They are sustained and fed by those derived formations. Therefore protective powers set a guard over them, to keep them until the hour when those governing falsities are stripped of those borrowed forms and are brought up again. They are gathered above and poured back into their first states, into the very characters in which they originally were. The remaining light within them is taken away. And they too are cast down into the same full series of outermost ways within the general knowledges outside the ordered mind, to the place where the former ones were cast. They enter into their midst again, and whatever light is in them is removed.

See now: instruction has been given about these obscuring states—how they rise and how the lives within them are purified—and also about the governing falsities within them—how they stir rebellions and how they are caught. They are cast by protective powers into the outer prison when the command comes through the power of manifested divine wisdom, who has authority over the whole intermediate domain and cleanses the life within it.

---

## Chapter 96: The Three Earths that Exist, they bear Fruit.

**¶1** Three earths exist in the universe, and are being toiled over. At first they [resist] the farmer in them. Afterwards they bear [true] fruit to they who toil over them.

**¶2** One of them is this great earth that people are living upon. They are ploughing in it [digging], it with iron hoes, they cultivate, [cutting] the furrows, turning it over [in order. Afterwards they harvest fruit from it] Afterwards they harvest fruit from [it ...

**¶3** ⟨p.245⟩
The second field is the living soul, the holy one. It is mixed in the entire zone, above and below. The farmers who toil over it are these two great light-givers, which journey through the heavens. They are toiling and cultivating it, so that they will draw it towards their aspect. And it leaps up and comes to the heights. It goes upwards, out from every place wherein it is, and it adds to them.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶1 [none]: Clean correspondential opening: the teaching is by function (earth resists cultivation, then bears fruit), setting up a threefold process. No administrative overlay or seam expansion is detectable her
> - ¶2 [none]: Genuine difficulty from damage/truncation, not insertion: the narrative breaks mid-agricultural description and again at the harvest clause. The voice remains correspondential throughout; the stumble 
> - ¶3 [none]: Clean correspondential development of the second ‘earth’: the ‘living soul’ is treated as a field by what happens to it (mixed throughout; worked by heavenly motions; drawn upward; adds to the lower).
> - Chapter-level (across ¶1–¶3) [none]: Unfinished triad is best explained as excerpt loss/fragmentation rather than a seam expansion or administrative insertion. The chapter promises “three earths,” but the surviving text gives only the fi

**Changes:** No changes made. All findings (1–4) recommended none (no excisions; difficulties attributed to damage/omission rather than later overlays).

**Spiritual narrative:** There are three distinct degrees in the whole of life that exist to be brought into order by continual operation. At first each degree stands opposed and unyielding to the formative work acting upon it; afterward, when it has been opened and made receptive, it brings forth genuine goods and truths as results for those who are devoted to uses.

One degree is the outermost plane of life in which people think, choose, and act. This plane is worked upon by firm, strong truths applied to resistant states, by which what is confused is set into an ordered pattern, what is hardened is broken up, and what lies closed is opened for reception. When this ordering has been effected, the outermost life yields actual goods in works as the harvest of that impressed order.

The second degree is the living interior in a person, the holy faculty that receives life itself. It is present throughout the whole person in what is higher and what is lower, within and without, because it is the common subject into which higher life flows. The powers that operate upon it are the two universal heavenly forces that give wisdom and love; they move through all spiritual states and work unceasingly so that this interior may be drawn away from self and turned toward their own quality. As this operation succeeds, the interior is lifted into a higher state, it withdraws from every lower confinement in which it had been held, and it becomes an added strength and enrichment to all the lower forms that depend upon it.

---

> - ¶2 [none]: Genuine difficulty from damaged/garbled Vorlage rather than a voice-insertion. The paragraph is supposed to introduce a threefold taxonomy of earthly/animal formation, but key terms are corrupt (“thre
> - ¶3 [excise]: Insertion into a functional (correspondential) discussion: a brief administrative/jurisdictional tag has been dropped into an otherwise correspondential account of threefold nourishment. The paragraph

**Changes:** Applied Finding 2 (¶3, excise): removed the intrusive administrative/jurisdictional tag “The Ambassador [members] all powers [of Light is established over].” No other paragraphs were altered per Findings 1, 3, and 4 (recommendation: none).

**Spiritual narrative:** Every affection that appears in the outer human, and every other formed quality in the ultimate degree, is fixed and made stable through the complete ordering of the three degrees of life. Some are brought forth from disordered beginnings that do not rightly receive influx, so that what comes to birth is a distorted determination. Others, however, take form by a more direct reception of their ruling love, and thus come into their own quality according to their kind.

Their sustenance is likewise threefold: they can be fed by inward wisdom, if they are willing to receive it; but if they are unwilling, they take in only truths that remain merely in potency and works that are pleasant outwardly. They appropriate these into themselves, and by that appropriation they express, in their own way, the three universal patterns of life from their inborn quality, and they abide in those patterns so long as they remain in the outer state. While they are immersed there, their ruling cravings seize them and carry them. The governing influx present in ultimates gathers all operative abilities under its disposal and arranges them for use.

Then, in the first ordering of the outer degree, the initial emergence of good and truth was set forth in a general and undistinguished way. From a contrary intention there arose harmful and bitter perceptions, together with stubborn persuasions, prickly defenses of selfhood, and other like falsities and evils in the outermost mind.

For such an intention belongs to dominions that delight in destroying what is innocent and living. Yet the milder and merely natural delights that were set in place belong to rudimentary and common things of the external life, because such delights are what they are in their own character. Nevertheless, that destructive intention, so far as it first shows itself in outwardly pleasant works and in rudimentary truth, and has not yet descended into the full profanation of will and understanding, also brings forth perceptions that yield works able to serve as nourishment, because those delights are then softened in externals.

But again, that destructive intention, when it flows from powers that both harm innocence and at the same time cling to outward forms of truth and to mild natural pleasures, produces perceptions that yield no nourishment. The works that proceed from them incline neither toward living good nor toward confirmed evil, and thus remain without a determinate spiritual life.

## Chapter 98: What is Virginal; or, otherwise, what is Continent?

**¶3** What is called ‘virginal’ is assigned to the mystery of the aeons of greatness. And they called it ‘virginal’ by the category of all the light that did not [taste] of death, nor come to the enemy’s war.

**¶4** However, what is called ‘continent’ occurs to the mystery of the light that came and was mixed with the darkness. It was defiled in bodies [in the garments of the flesh] and went up to the light. [They] called [it ‘co]ntinent’.

**¶5** Furthermore, the holy vir[gin]als [when they stand in their purity] should they come out against the en[emy and shall ... not] taste death, nor [be overcome by him].

**¶6** ⟨p.249⟩ On the other hand, what is called ‘continent’ [is this li]ght that mixed with the darkness, was purif[ie]d, came [up f]rom all the creatures, and was confirmed in the great[ness]. They called it ‘continent’ since it tasted [of the l]ust; but afterwards was cleansed from it and [become an] holy one.

**¶7** Furthermore, what is called [‘vi]rginal’ is this light that was purified by the ima[ge] of the [A]mbassador. It wen[t up and] attained [the] he[ights. It was] confirmed in the image of the gods.

**¶8** Again, what is [called] ‘continent’ is the remainder of this light th[at] remained behind, from what was cleansed. It goes up and comes down in transmigration. Al[so], there is a part [of] it that shall come and attain the form of [hum]an [fl]esh. Again, that one too shall be chosen in the flesh, [accept] the hope, and become an holy one. And they name it ‘continent’.

**¶9** Furthermore, what is named ‘virginal’ is the Light Mind, should he come and assume the images [of] the ele[ct]. However, on the other hand, what is called ‘contin[ent]’ is the new man, should it be purified from this old man. And it is cleansed and strips off the Sin that is compounded with it, and it becomes a continent one.

**¶10** And also, what is called ‘virgi[n]al’ in the flesh, is a man if he has [ne]ver joined himself to woman, has not been defiled by intercourse. However, [i]n contrast, what is called ‘continent’, is the man who has a wom[a]n in the world. Afterwards, he cleanses himself from her [and] renounces her. And because of this he [turns back from her] and he becomes an holy contin[en]t one.

**¶11** Behold, I have taught you the categories [of] the v[ir]ginal and the continent in a great many [as]pects. For what is virginal, or otherwise what is [con]tinent, is in many forms.

> - ¶7 [annotate]: Mixed voice at a seam: the teaching is still about purification/elevation (substrate), but it is carried by identity-titles (“Ambassador,” “gods”) that do not arise from functional description and shi
> - ¶8 [none]: Genuine doctrinal/lexical difficulty rather than an insertion: ‘transmigration’ introduces a different explanatory mechanism, but it is not administrative (no jurisdictions/ranks), and it directly con
> - ¶9-10 [none]: Clean correspondential internalization into anthropology/ethics (new man/old man; virginity/continence). The register shifts, but it is a normal late-step application from cosmic process to personal p

**Changes:** No changes made. The only targeted issue was Finding 4 (¶7) with an ANNOTATE recommendation; per instructions, annotation does not alter the paragraph text. All other findings recommended none, so the chapter remains unchanged.

**Spiritual narrative:** The state called pure belongs to the hidden arcana of the highest heaven. It is called pure because it is the whole quality of wisdom that has never been tainted by evil, nor entered into combat with hell.

The state called self-restrained belongs to the arcana of wisdom that did descend into obscurity and was mingled with falsity. In that mingling it was profaned in external forms of life, yet afterward it was raised again into wisdom; therefore it is called self-restrained.

Those who are fully purified, when they stand against hell, do not admit evil into themselves, nor are they overcome by it; they remain untouched by spiritual death.

Self-restraint, however, is wisdom that was mingled with falsity and afterward purified, gathered up out of disordered lower affections, and fixed again in the higher heaven. It is called self-restrained because it first admitted the delight of evil, but afterward was cleansed of that delight and made holy.

Purity is also wisdom purified by a Divine pattern of mediation: it is raised into more interior degrees and confirmed in a formed likeness of heavenly order.

Self-restraint is likewise the remaining portion of that wisdom which, after purification, still lingers in lower things: it rises and descends through successive changes of state. Part of it also comes to be embodied in the outermost human life; and even that, when it is chosen in that outer life, receives faith in deliverance and becomes holy, and so is named self-restrained.

Purity is the Divine Understanding when it comes and takes on the formed qualities of those who are being saved. Self-restraint is the new will and understanding in a person when it is purified from the former self: it is cleansed, casts off the evil conjoined with it, and becomes self-restrained.

Even in the outermost life, purity is a person whose love has never been bound to profaning delights and has not been defiled by their conjunction; but self-restraint is a person who has lived with such delights, and afterward is cleansed from them and renounces them, and by this turning away is made holy.

These are the kinds of purity and self-restraint in many aspects, for purity and self-restraint exist in many forms.

---

But when the leading falsity is forcibly restrained so it can no longer act or persuade, the dependent powers that had taken their coherence from it are torn apart and lose their joined operation. ## Chapter 100: Concerning the Dragon with Fourteen Heads; what it is and [...]

**¶2** There is never a dragon in the flesh with fourteen heads, as the Magi say! Rather, this is a spiritual lesson. It was uttered in virginity [in a state of purity] the Magi did not understand [because they remained in outward knowing] all their words that are written [spiritual]tual (?).

**¶3** Again, this is how this earth [is likened] to it, as it is accounted a corporeal dragon [and a great deceit is joined] I will instruct you [about this] the dragon is this doctrine [of life] of the flesh [that is from the self] also the fourteen heads that are distributed over the dragon are these.

**¶4** The seven sense organs on the head of the body are the two of sight by which one sees; the two of sound by which one hears; the two of scent by which one smells; also the tongue [which is in the mouth] by which one selects, receiving there the different tastes that occur in every form.

**¶5** These are the seven heads of the dragon, the seven sense organs that are on the upper part of the person; [and the seven are] below. The seven of the torso are these: two [of drawing in and expelling]; the two [of moving and acting] ruler [in the midst, the] the seven heads that are above; so with the ones below they total and make fourteen heads of the dragon.

**¶6** ⟨p.253⟩
Conversely, the five dens about which they have spoken, where the dragon is congregated and protrudes, are these: the first is the tongue; the second is the lungs; the third is the heart; the fourth is the spleen; the fifth is the blood that dwells in them. Again, if it shall look out from them [and reveal its likeness and its observation above and below, it wages war by] its fourteen heads.

**¶7** Whoever will recognise them with the dragon, which is the thought of the body, and struggles with it and is victorious and kills it in them [and uproots it from his members.] He receives the victory without suffering on the day of his coming forth.

**¶8** So, behold, I have taught you of the dragon that exists with its fourteen heads and five dens, wherein it is concealed and hidden. It does through them [its desire to rule]ire all the time.
