**Spiritual narrative:** No monstrous evil exists as an outward reality in the way the unillumined suppose; the image was given as spiritual instruction. It was spoken from a state of pure reception, yet those who remained in external knowing did not grasp the inward meaning of what was set down.

The teaching concerns the natural degree when it is taken as the whole of life and so becomes a ruling falsity joined with persuasion. This ruling falsity is a life-principle from the self-sense, and the many dominant agencies by which it governs are meant by the many heads.

First are the complete set of outward inlets through which the natural mind receives appearances and judges by them: the twofold reception of visual impressions, the twofold reception of auditory impressions, the twofold reception of olfactory impressions, and the discriminating reception that sorts and chooses according to relish, taking in innumerable variations as they present themselves.

These are the chief outward inlets in the higher region of the natural. Beneath them are the corresponding inward-acting powers in the lower region that execute and confirm what the higher inlets suggest: a twofold power that takes in and casts out as to natural vitality, a twofold power that initiates and performs as to natural strength, and a central governing power that presides in the midst and supports the whole working of what is above. Together the higher inlets and the lower executors make a full sufficiency of dominant agencies by which the self-derived rule governs both what is received and what is done.

In the opposite description, the hidden interiors where that ruling falsity gathers and from which it thrusts itself outward are: the delight of sensuous discrimination that claims authority to decide by what pleases; the general sustaining motion of natural thought by which its activity is kept continual; the central natural love that gives heat to intentions; the obscure hoarding support that hardens what is unclean and unexamined; and the common life that circulates through these interiors, carrying their quality into every function. When that hidden rule looks outward from those interiors and displays itself in higher reception and lower execution, it fights by the whole sufficiency of its dominant agencies.

Whoever recognizes these workings as one with the bodily-minded thought that would rule, and resists it, and prevails until its dominion in him is ended, comes forth into freedom without the suffering that otherwise attends that conflict when the outer life is laid aside.

This is the instruction: that the ruling falsity of life from the self is present with a complete sufficiency of governing agencies and with interior seats where it hides, and through these it continually drives its craving to rule.

---

## Chapter 101: [Concer]ning why, if the Person shall look down into Water, [ ... ]

**¶1** instruct me about this lesson: When the person is above water and looking down [into the wat]ers, if he shall see his face reflected why shall you find his head turned upside down and also his feet upwards? Yet, when he stares down into the waters, his face shall not be visible, nor can he see it there?

**¶3** [And] this: The face of people and the shape of trees shall appear turned upside down in the water as this is signified in [the] mystery of the summons, when it was sent down to the worlds of darkness towards the First Man. Sin[ce] in this way it cast itself down [to the worlds of darkness] like a person [who] plunges headlong into water; so it is also with the summons. It cast itself headlong to the worlds below, with strength and diligence.

**¶4** And this too: Shall the person lift his head up from the water, his face shall not be visible to him, being bent below. This characterises the mystery of the obedience that ascended from below, from with the First Man his father to the Living Spirit. This is a spiritual mystery that I have [revea]led to you so that you would understand it.

**¶5** If you wish to understand another corporeal mystery on this subject, listen and I may teach you to understand it. The face of people and of animals and of trees is visible in the water, hanging upside down. This occurs to the mystery of the stars and the zodiac, which hang upside down and are visible in the great sea. For the face of people and beasts and all trees hang on the root of the stars and the zodiac, being begotten from them. Just as their fathers, who are spread out above, hang upside down; so it is also that the mystery of their fathers is being revealed to them whenever they stare down into the water. This is, that you shall find their shapes inverted.

**¶6** ⟨p.255⟩
Not only this, but [the] child who is born; as the ho[ur] approaches for him to be born, he hangs upside down and is born head first. Indeed, due to this, his mystery shall be revealed in these waters people stare down into.

**¶7** Furthermore, I reveal something else to you: Behold, you will find the shadow of the person who walks along is joined to the earth all the time. If the person is troubled [in his mind] his shadow up upon the earth [becomes strong] above. It is impossible for him to do [anything good] on the earth. See, this mystery is [a] great sign, signifying that the entire body came from the earth and had ascended from the abyss. Again, due to this, its shadow is joined on the earth, turned downwards, and hanging from above.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶1 [none]: Clean correspondential opening: a natural optical phenomenon is used to teach by function (higher/lower standpoint yields inversion; fixation on the lower yields no true self-knowledge). No narrative 
> - ¶3 [none]: Minor mixed/unclear agency (doublet) inside an otherwise correspondential descent motif: “summons” is made to act like a person plunging headlong, but it is also positioned within a mythic administrat
> - ¶4 [none]: Clean correspondential ascent-counterpart to ¶3: the functional logic is symmetrical (lifting head from water → image disappears; ascent of obedience from below → return movement). The named figure “L
> - ¶5-¶6 [none]: Clean correspondential exemplification (cosmic and embryological) expanding the same inversion principle by functional dependence and ‘hanging’: reflection shows things “hanging upside down”; stars/zo
> - ¶7 [none]: Genuine difficulty only in damaged spots, not an insertion: admonitory/moral application of the same ‘downward adhesion’ theme (shadow clings to earth; mental trouble strengthens shadow; inability to 

**Changes:** No changes made. The critic’s findings (Summary; Findings 1–5) explicitly recommend “none” throughout and state that no passage meets criteria for excision; therefore the restored chapter text is retained unchanged.

**Spiritual narrative:** Teach me the inward sense of this lesson: when a person is raised into a clearer discernment of truth and from that height examines the lower understanding where truths lie in general form and are mixed with obscurities, what he then perceives about himself in that lower plane appears inverted, as though what ought to rule were serving and what ought to serve were ruling. Yet when he fixes his attention on that lower plane itself as if it were the whole of truth, his genuine quality is not disclosed there, and he cannot recognize himself from that level.

Also teach me this: the inverted appearance of human quality and of perceptive form within the lower understanding is bound up with the secret of a Divine incitement and awakening that was sent down into states of falsity to meet the good that enters directly into conflict with evil. That awakening let itself down into the lower degree as if it had wholly cast itself into it; in the same way the Divine call descended into the states beneath with power, persistence, and unwearied application.

Teach me this too: when a person lifts his interior sight away from that lower plane, he cannot then see his own quality in it, because what had been appearing belongs to the lower degree and remains below the present regard. This discloses the secret of obedience returning upward: submission and reception rising from the lower degree—from within the good that was in combat—toward the living operation of the Divine. This is a spiritual secret made known so that it may be understood.

If one wishes to grasp another outward-seeming secret on the same subject, let him consider this: the human quality, the affections of life, and the forms of perception can be seen within general external truth as though suspended and reversed. This is because the higher ordering of knowledges and their fixed patterns, when viewed from the plane of common externals, appear contrary to their own origin. Human qualities, affections, and perceptions depend upon that higher ordering and are derived from it; therefore, whenever one looks into general external truth, he perceives an image of higher causes in a reversed order, and so something of the hidden nature of their origin is disclosed by that inversion.

Nor is this the only instance: even the beginning of a new human state, when it is approaching its outward manifestation, is first arranged in a contrary orientation, and it enters the outer life by what is primary; therefore that secret too is mirrored in general external truths, where beginnings present themselves in a reversed form.

Further, this also is revealed: the mere attendant appearance that follows a person in his progress clings continually to what is lowest and cannot separate from it; and if the person is inwardly troubled and disordered, that attendant appearance becomes more dominant in the outermost life, so that he cannot act rightly there. This is a great sign that the whole outward person is from the lowest degree and has been brought up from the extreme natural level; therefore the attendant appearance remains bound to what is lowest, facing downward, as something hanging from what is higher and having no life from itself.

---

## Chapter 102: Concerning the Light Mind, why it does not exercise Foreknowledge for the Saint as for the Apostle?

**¶2** Happen you know that the Light Mind, who dwells in the elect, has the power to perform all these wonders among the faithful, all these signs that you have recounted to me; but the elect are not able to persist in the greatness that may be unveiled to them.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶2 [none]: Clean correspondential (functional) teaching: the text explains efficacy vs. receptivity (power to act vs. inability to persist). No narrative seam, no list of jurisdictions, no governance taxonomy. T

**Changes:** No changes made. Finding 1 (¶2) recommendation: none.

**Spiritual narrative:** Know that enlightened understanding, living within those who are in genuine good, holds the power to bring forth every spiritual work and every confirmation of truth within those who receive it. Yet those who are being reformed cannot remain steadily in the loftier openings that are granted to them, because their state is not yet fixed enough to bear that elevation without relapse.

It implants this full set of operations in the regenerated person after separating them from defilement and falsity, sustaining the new life by steady influx, and distinguishing them for conjunction with what is Divine.

## Chapter 104: Concerning Food: It shall be allocated to Five Products of the human Body.

**¶2** This sustenance of various kinds that people gather in and eat, and enters the body; it shall be distributed to five productions.

**¶3** The first product is this that shall issue from the person in rapture and rises up in the Mind, and it comes out from all his limbs, and is immeasurable.

**¶4** The second is this that shall issue from the person in voice and word.

**¶5** The third is this that shall spring from them in strength and activity.

**¶6** The fourth is what is engendered by the pleasure of lu[s]t in men and women.

**¶7** The fifth is this that shall be formed and sculpted in the flesh, and be engendered and come from them. It is this corporeal creature; only its parents can recognise this creature that they beget. They are attentive to its desire and its thought and its [lo]ve every day, all the time. However, these other four forms they neither notice nor are concerned about; because they are not displayed.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶2 [none]: Clean correspondential setup: ‘food’ is treated functionally as what is taken in and becomes the body’s substance, then mapped to a fivefold sequence of outcomes (‘productions’). No intrusion of named
> - ¶3-¶5 [none]: Clean, internally graded functional sequence (interior → expression → execution). The ‘rapture/Mind/limbs’ triad reads as a correspondential anatomy of states and operations rather than an administrat
> - ¶6 [none]: Genuine difficulty only in moral ‘shift,’ not a textual intrusion. The fourth item introduces sexual lust as a lower generative mode; this is still functional (a production generated by a specific app
> - ¶7 [none]: Clean correspondential climax and perceptibility-contrast. The fifth production is bodily offspring; the closing contrast (‘only its parents recognize it… the other four forms… not displayed’) functio

**Changes:** No changes made. The critic’s findings for ¶2–¶7 all recommended none (no excisions or annotations), judging the chapter coherent and free of intrusive overlay material.

**Spiritual narrative:** All the instruction, affection, and knowledge that a person receives and makes their own within life is taken into the inner human and, from that appropriation, is set in order into a sufficient series of outflowing effects.

The first effect is an outflow that arises when the person is lifted above the ordinary state into interior perception, and it ascends into the ruling understanding. It then diffuses through all the powers of life in the whole person, and its extension cannot be measured because it reaches as far as the person’s reception allows.

The second effect is an outflow that comes forth as communicated truth, when what is within passes into expressed meaning and ordered utterance.

The third effect is an outflow that becomes actual power for use, when inward intention passes into vigor, execution, and accomplished works.

The fourth effect is an outflow generated from merely natural delight separated from higher love, when the will is kindled by impure pleasure and seeks conjunction on that level.

The fifth effect is an outflow fixed in the outermost degree as a new formed life in the natural plane, brought into determinate existence from its origins. This is life in the lowest level, and only those who were the immediate source of it distinctly acknowledge it as theirs. They attend to its tendencies, its thinking, and its love continually, without ceasing; yet the other four effects they do not perceive and do not care for, because these do not appear outwardly in the senses.

---

## Chapter 106: There is no Joy that shall remain in the World till the End.

**¶1** There is not [o]ne person [existing in] the world, standing firm [i]n mixture, for whom joy remains to the end.

**¶2** Not only people, even the sun and the moon, the light-givers of the heavens, [are set in their courses] nor are they oppressed, they know not [to be set]et firm in a summons and an [obe]dience. Nevertheless, they too, they do not rejoice completely.

**¶3** So, one: Their eyes stare at their enemy, they [perceiv]e him and also look at his ugly shapes. They [s]ee him, that he is alive and established and is king above and below, in secret and manifest. He does every thing that he wants according to his wil[l].

**¶4** [The s]econd: They perceive the living soul, they see [i]t and that it is ensnared and set in a great [fetter] above and below, in the tree and in the flesh, [bound] with every oppression. It is being pressed, drawn near to [and] sliced and eaten as it comes up and down; from above below and from below above. It [is] despoiled and moved from body to body.

**¶5** Now, because of their living soul that is loved by them and honoured of them, when they see it in oppression th[ey can] not [re]joice completely.

**¶6** So, one about the soul; and one about their enemy whom they see ruling [in] what is not his own. He is proud and satisfied with a wealth that is not his own. Indeed, due to this, they do not rejoice in total; but bear up and swallow their heart until they cleanse the living soul and take it from the enemy's hands. Then they obliterate the death that is their enemy and they gather it in [and shut it up in the lump] for ever and ever.

**¶7** When they will chain their enemy in the bond at the [las]t, then they will rejoice and no longer grieve from this t[ime]. Also, they become the very same as they were from the [start] for ever!

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶1 [none]: Clean opening thesis in correspondential mode: joy cannot remain while existence is ‘standing firm in mixture.’ No administrative overlay; the statement sets a functional/spiritual condition (mixture 
> - ¶2 [none]: Minor idiomatic/lexical difficulty (institutional phrasing) but not a true voice-insertion. The line reads like a Manichaean cosmic-regularity claim applied to sun/moon; ‘summons and obedience’ is awk
> - ¶3-¶5 [none]: Clean twofold correspondential teaching (first grief/second grief) with functional spiritual psychology: seeing the enemy’s activity and seeing the soul’s captivity causes incomplete joy. No narrative
> - ¶4 [none]: Genuine difficulty from dense, possibly damaged or culturally-specific captivity imagery (‘tree and flesh’, ‘sliced and eaten’). The reader had to ‘best-fit’ these phrases, but removing them would not
> - ¶6 [none]: Idiomatic seam (‘swallow their heart’; ‘shut it up in the lump’) but still continuous with the chapter’s deliverance arc. No administrative overlay: the passage keeps agency with the same actors (‘the
> - ¶7 [none]: Clean conclusion: final binding of the enemy and restoration to the primordial state. This resolves the opening thesis and the two griefs without introducing foreign material.

**Changes:** No changes made. The textual criticism findings recommended no excisions or annotations for ¶1–¶7 (Findings 1–6: all ‘none’).

**Spiritual narrative:** No one in the created order can remain in a settled conjunction of good and truth in such a mixed state that full blessedness endures without interruption to the completion.

Even the universal powers that give light to understanding and warmth to love are held in their appointed order without compulsion, for they do not resist the rule above them; yet they too cannot be in unbroken fullness of delight while opposition still stands within the domain.

For there is a first grief: discernment looks steadily upon the reigning evil, perceives it, and sees its repulsive forms. It recognizes that this evil is active and confirmed, exercising dominion in inward and outward things, secretly and openly, doing all it wills from its own love.

And there is a second grief: discernment perceives the living spiritual principle and sees it caught under a great constraint in inner and outer degrees, held within perverted perception and within corrupt life, bound by continual oppression. It is pressed, drawn into proximity, divided and consumed as it rises and falls through states; it is driven from higher to lower and from lower to higher, stripped of coverings and carried from one external form to another.

Because this living spiritual principle is loved and held in reverence, those who love it cannot rejoice completely while they behold it under such oppression.

Thus there is sorrow both for the captive life within, and for the hostile evil seen ruling what is not its own. That evil exults and is satisfied with good and truth it has seized as if they were its possession. Therefore joy is not yet total, but there is endurance and inward restraint until the living spiritual principle is purified and taken out of the enemy’s dominion. Then the intention of spiritual death, which is the enemy, is deprived of its power and confined so it cannot go forth again.

When, at the completion, the enemy is finally bound so it cannot act, joy becomes full and grief no longer returns. Then the state is restored in permanence to what it was in its first purity, to eternity.

---

## Chapter 107: Concerning the Form of the Word, that [ ... 30 ... ]

**¶1** [The mouth takes it and the throat draws it up and the tongue spreads it out and the teeth cut it and the lips gather it!] and the throat draws it up and the tongue spreads it out and the teeth cut it and the lips g[at]her it! The word shall come forth through the power of th[ese five] members and be heard outside.

**¶2** Simila[rly] the coin: One shall pour it out and an [othe]r beat it and another trim it as it is turned, and a[nother] put the stamp on it and another wipe it in the sieve (?). [Behold], these five craftsmen shall shape and beautify [their] coin, and it comes amongst mankind. It becomes a posse[ssion] to be received and given.

**¶3** This is also the case with the [wor]d, as it is formed and embellished by five [member]s. It comes forth and is heard by the ears [of] others.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶1 [none]: Clean correspondential teaching with a minor scribal/duplicative disturbance: the five-member sequence is accidentally repeated (dittography), but the passage’s functional logic remains intact.
> - ¶2 [none]: Clean correspondential analogy (craft production → communicable ‘coin’), with one localized lexical uncertainty (“sieve (?)”) that creates a small interpretive stumble but not a narrative/voice break.
> - ¶3 [none]: Clean closure and generalization that resumes the chapter’s single functional voice; no disruption.

**Changes:** No changes made. All findings (¶1–¶3) carried a “none” recommendation, with no excisions required.

**Spiritual narrative:** Inner meaning is first received into conscious expression; then it is lifted into clearer awareness; then discerning intelligence unfolds it into ordered thought; then judgment divides and selects what can serve use; and then the outer formation gathers it into a unified utterance suited to reception. Thus communicated meaning comes forth by a sufficient complement of operative powers and enters the external plane where it can be perceived by others.

In the same way, a truth meant for common life is brought from an unformed state into a usable one through a sufficient series of distinct operations: one operation supplies and yields the matter for formation, another consolidates and strengthens it, another sets it into right measure as it is carried into outward form, another impresses on it a determinate quality and authority from its ruling love, and another purifies it in the ultimate so that it is clean for presentation. By these sufficient operations, truth is shaped and made fitting and attractive, and then it enters communal use, becoming something that can be appropriated, held, communicated, and exchanged.

So it is with every meaning that is to be communicated: it is first formed within, then prepared for reception through a sufficient complement of operative powers, until it comes forth into external perception and is received by the understanding of others.

---

## Chapter 108: Concerning the Seed Grain that shall be formed by the Elements, and also be destroyed by them

**¶2** “This seed grain and this barley grain that you see shall be formed and [be gathered in] and beautified by the five elements. Now, the warmth and the cold nourish the seed grain and the entire tree. So, just as these things nourish it [and increase, and just as they strengthen it] it is destroyed [when they withdraw from it] through [the lack of nourishment] hunger.

**¶4** “In this way too, a lack and a shortfall shall occur from place to place through them; and they who have chosen it have gathered it in and beautified and attained it, while an affliction shall arise for it through those.

**¶5** “People also shall be caused to stumble on their account, through the energy of Matter that dwells in them. It has poured its shadow over them, just as it shall also pour its shadow over the elements and destroy the fruits of all the trees.”

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶2 [none]: Coherent correspondential substrate: the teaching proceeds by function (nourishment/withdrawal → growth/destruction) using a natural process (seed/tree) to model spiritual formation and deprivation. T
> - ¶4 [none]: Coherent continuation in the same correspondential voice: the teaching remains functional (lack/shortfall propagating “from place to place”; chosen/appropriated deficiency; distress arising from the s
> - ¶5 [none]: Mixed but non-disruptive: the paragraph introduces a Manichaean technical term (“energy of Matter”) that can signal later doctrinal layering, yet here it functions causally (Matter’s energy → stumblin

**Changes:** No changes made. All findings (1–3) recommended none; there were no excisions to execute and no annotate recommendations that would affect the clean text.

**Spiritual narrative:** A living truth in potency, together with the understanding of good that attends it, is brought into determinate form, gathered into the mind, and given an appearance of order through a sufficient series of fundamental states in the outer degree. Alternations of affection and obscurity sustain that truth in potency and sustain the whole formed perception that grows from it; yet as these alternations can support reception and growth, so they can also fail. When sustaining influx is withdrawn, the capacity to receive is laid waste by deprivation, and spiritual life collapses into want.

So too, through such disordered states, deficiencies and lapses arise successively through the particulars of life. Those who have chosen that disorder take such deficiency into themselves, confirm it, and adorn it until it appears complete; yet distress arises within it from the very influences by which it was established.

Because of this, many are made to fall away, for the lower self that loves what is merely its own dwells within them and impels them. That self spreads obscurity over their understanding, and it spreads the same obscurity over foundational truths in the outer mind, until the works that should proceed from sound perception are ruined throughout.

---

And the summons with the obedience [constitute] twenty-five characteristics in their twenty-five limbs, [which] are the sons of the Man. The summons and [the obedience]ience were raised to the heights through them.

**¶3** ⟨p.263⟩ Just as their fa[ther] the living Man raised himself up through them, by these these other ones have set up all things a[ove]e and below. Indeed, these are what are called pentecost, the fifty great days that are the [archetypes of the] holy [d]ays. They fasted from their [foo]d, [w]hich is their own.

**¶4** [*] the glorious one who came [of]heir food which is the [living food] of sou[l], which is the summons and the obedience [life] that spiritual food. which [through] this living food suffices for all of them. It filled them all with power and richness and life. Light [for] this living food that [by] became rich.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶2 [none]: Largely clean correspondential substrate: the teaching proceeds by functional relations (summons/answer; descent/ascent; ordered powers/limbs) and generates the spiritual sense without needing jurisdi
> - ¶3 [none]: Clean continuation of the same correspondential process, with a small seam where a calendrical identification is appended (“pentecost, the fifty great days”). This looks like a liturgical/glossary-sty
> - ¶4 [excise]: Insertion/expansion at a seam: the narrative shifts from an atemporal functional cycle into a dated salvation-historical timeline (“from that time until… the advent of Jesus the Splendour”) and contai
> - ¶2-¶4 (overall) [none]: No administrative overlay block is present: there are no ranked lists of authorities, jurisdictions, camps, watches, or stations. Even the numeration (5, 25, 50) is embedded in functional process (pow

No other paragraphs were altered (Findings 1, 2, and 4 recommended no action).

**Spiritual narrative:** Know that within each external truth by which the Divine first works in the outermost degree there dwells a sufficient set of interior faculties. These faculties are brought into order in relation to one another until their sufficiency is completed in fullness.

When directed influx descended into a lower degree, the receptive answering within what received it at the same time yearned to rise toward its source. Directed influx together with compliant reception brought forth a full series of determinate spiritual qualities in a full series of distinct forms of action; these are the derivative powers proceeding from the Divine in its engagement with what stands in the way. Through these derivative powers, directed influx and compliant reception were lifted into higher states, because these powers supplied the ordered forms by which elevation could be effected.

As the Divine good that first enters combat with opposition raised itself by means of these ordered operative forms, so by those same forms everything in higher and lower degrees was set in order and held together. This is the complete cycle of a full spiritual process, the archetypal pattern of all states made holy in regeneration. In that cycle they abstained from what is merely their own, refusing to take their life from selfhood.

From then onward, until the coming of Divine truth in revealed glory, the nourishment of their inner life was the living provision of the soul: directed influx joined with compliant reception. Through the whole fullness of that complete cycle they were sustained by it, because that living nourishment was sufficient for all of them. It filled them with operative strength, with abundance, and with spiritual vitality; and by it the understanding was illumined and all within them was enriched.

---

**¶1** exists [a power] daily to the body of the person [and] there are two hundred and fifty thousand seals in it [and] there are another two hundred and fifty thousand rulers [into the Interior] they are cleansed and purified and enter [rest] ⟨p.265⟩ by the voice, by the word, and by the [sound of the hymn] quiet with the silence at the time when his heart [is turned toward] of God the Father.

**¶2** [*] 

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶1 [none]: Core correspondential substrate: a functional process of nourishment/purification/admission accomplished by “voice/word/hymn/silence” when the heart turns to the Father. No narrative break; the spirit
> - ¶1 (opening numeration: “two hundred and fifty thousand seals… two hundred and fifty thousand rulers”) [none]: Genuine difficulty (quantitative hyperbole + uncertain technical terms), but not an insertion: the numbers do not introduce a different teaching mode; they remain embedded in the same purification/adm
> - ¶2 [excise]: Insertion/administrative intrusion into a functional purification narrative: a sudden mythic-political catalog item (“the twelve great archontic rulers… of the Darkness”) with exile/judgment mechanics

**Spiritual narrative:** There is an unceasing power that sustains a person’s outward life, and within that outward life there is an immense fullness of distinct confirmations and an equal fullness of governing determinations. These are successively set into a state fit for inward reception: what is impure is removed, what is disordered is put into order, and they are admitted into a quieter interior abiding. This is brought about through living influx received as discernment and doctrine, and through ordered utterance that carries understanding; and it is completed by a tempering peace that stills lower motions, when the inmost intention is turned toward the Divine Source.

Moreover, the full organized set of chief governing falsities that belong to denial is expelled from the inner domain and cast into instability, so that it passes through successive changes of state and reception. In these changes, some falsities are broken up and brought to nothing by the very alternations they undergo, while others are returned and re-formed when they can be bent away from opposition and reduced into a subordinate place.

---

## Chapter 111: Concerning the Four Archetypes that occur in the Eye, and the Fifth that is hidden in them; to whom do they belong?

**¶1** There are four aspects among mankind that perceive, as they perceive [in them] without these four that are hidden in them [and perceive] they belong [to the rest of mankind]. So, [if one is lacking, perception is broken].

**¶2** [The desire] that is in the [heart] belongs to the fire. The darkness belongs to the abyss; but the light that perceives what is hidden in all these things, being clear, is the living air.

**¶3** How shall the pupil of the eye perceive? While it perceives by day, yet come the night it shall not perceive. What mystery does this matter signify? The pupil of the eye shall not [enter into] the mystery of the [hidden things] is the door [of the mystery] below [that is] the other four cardinal points. For in the [the compass of] above. He has [place] great [a] they came down [power] living [powers]. The First Man came forth from it and knew all things.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶1 [none]: Clean correspondential opening: the chapter asserts a functional fourfold interior basis for perception; the difficulty is only syntactic damage, not a voice shift or intrusion.
> - ¶2 [none]: Genuine difficulty: the chapter promises four, but this paragraph explicitly names only three items (desire/heart→fire; darkness→abyss; perceiving light→living air). This is likely lacuna/abrasion rat
> - ¶3 [none]: Damaged middle producing forced narrative reconstruction: the paragraph begins coherently (pupil/day-night), then breaks into fragmentary spatial/cosmological phrases (“door… below… other four cardina

**Changes:** No changes made. All findings (1–3) recommended none (no excisions, no annotations), so the restored text is retained verbatim.

**Spiritual narrative:** Within the human race there is a fourfold completeness of inner faculties by which a person can have perception as perception; and though this completeness lies concealed within, without it the rest of the mind cannot truly enter the human order that is meant for heaven, so perception fails and becomes fragmented.

The longing that stirs in the inmost love belongs to the warmth of the will. The obscuring falsity that swallows discernment belongs to the lowest disorder where truth is lost. But the clear intelligence that can notice what is concealed within every such state and its workings is the living breath of understanding.

How can the receptive point of discernment perceive? While it is in illumination it does perceive; but when obscurity comes over it, it does not. This teaches a hidden point: that receptive discernment cannot by itself pass into the interior arcanum, because entrance into the deeper degree is through an opening beneath the whole range and fullness of the outer mind, while the interior degree itself is above the external arrangement. From that higher degree there descended a great fullness of living powers as an influx into lower states; and from that influx the primal heavenly human within a person came into activity and thereby knew all things.

---

**¶4** However, [the i]dol of ill-fated human[it]y [set itself up] the one that will be unlike anything, that is equal [to the power and the essence] great works; and it was found evil [by] God, the Lord of all. It even blasphemed again[st the] power that supports the totality, against its hol[y] light; [and it] profaned its glorious wisdom, bringing forth the cause of every sin. Even so, he sent the apostles to the good, [g]enera- tion by generation, and revealed to them five great thin[gs].

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶2 [none]: Coherent correspondential teaching using an impossible comparison to establish disproportion between “words of the flesh” and a higher reality. No clear narrative break; the difficulty is lexical (“fi
> - ¶3 [none]: Damaged and internally dislocated transmission rather than a clean doctrinal insertion. The paragraph contains at least two broken similes spliced together (sea-drowning and mountains-on-earth) and se
> - ¶4 [none]: Coherent moral-theological polemic continuous with the end of ¶3 (“no cause of evil after God”). The ‘rulers/demons/fiends/satans’ are not administrative jurisdictions here; they function as classes o
> - ¶7 [annotate]: Mixed voice: a likely seam where christological-historical narration (“Jesus… Adam and Eve… apostles”) has been fitted onto an earlier functional teaching about universal restoration/revelation. The s
> - ¶9-¶11 [none]: Clean enumerative teaching with minimal narrative friction: a list of revealed ‘things’ (aeons/darkness; light versus darkness; descent to the province of death). While list-like, it is not administra
> - ¶13 [none]: Truncated ending / missing continuation rather than insertion. It promises “three great mighty things” but the excerpt does not supply them, producing an incomplete closure that the spiritual reading 

**Changes:** No changes made. The critic issued no excisions: Findings 1, 2, 3, 5, and 6 recommend none, and Finding 4 recommends annotate (not text modification).

**Spiritual narrative:** When he came into a state where living truth flowed through the center of his understanding, he rejected the persuasion of merely outward life. He perceived that if the whole sufficient series of outer expressions arising from the natural self were brought up out of the deepest obscurity into the outermost plane, and if they were assembled into one seeming unity and arranged into a coherent order there, they would still not attain anything of what is truly elevated in the Lord. They could not be raised into the same interior degree, nor be extended into the same fullness, nor be opened into the same breadth, nor be brought down into the same complete ultimate, because they do not have the Divine quality within them.

And not only so: even if every one of those outer expressions, from the least to the greatest, were gathered into one general mass of knowledges and made to appear as a single comprehensive whole, they would still be absorbed into that generality, and their distinct form would perish there, as though extinguished in the merely natural plane. Yet within the natural plane there can still be many elevated states made great in outward form; and in their better condition these do not rise up against the holy stillness of Divine order, and they do not speak denial against the wisdom that enlightens, nor against the Divine power that holds all things together, nor against the Divine reality that is manifest through all things and gives them their total form, nor against the goods that proceed from righteousness; and in that better condition they do not invent an origin of evil in the Divine.

But the self-exalting principle of ruined humanity, which sets up what is unlike the Divine as though it were equal to Divine power and Divine being, was judged evil by the Lord of all. It spoke denial against the Divine power that supports the whole and against holy wisdom that gives light, and it violated Divine intelligence in its glory, and from that violation it produced the source of sin. From this came the blasphemous persuasion that every shameful evil and every defilement—every dominant evil, every hellish persuasion, every destroying lust, and every adversarial falsity—comes from the Divine, as though the Lord had established them from the beginning; yet they do not come from him, and this is a falsified witness against the Divine.

The Lord did not enter into the outermost degree and restore it for the sake of the human race alone, but in order to make manifest in ultimates what is powerful beyond ultimates, and to bring the whole expanse of created degrees into right order. When he finished his work in the outermost degree of universal life, he advanced further, conjoining the first formed good and the first formed truth to himself, and unveiling to them what had been hidden. In the same way he continually sent those who would teach to those capable of receiving good, age after age, and he disclosed to them a sufficient set of great essentials.

He opened to them the ordered successions of heavenly states and the manner in which they come forth, and he also taught the mode by which obscurity and falsity are permitted to exist. He announced to them the greatness of Divine wisdom, how it has acted against the strength of falsity and has overcome it. He taught that he entered into the state of spiritual death that reigns in the merely natural self, until he found what could be helped, and lifted them out of the lowest captivity in which they had been held.

All who are faithful and who inwardly acknowledge this truth receive spiritual life from three great and mighty essentials.

---

## Chapter 113: The Chapter on whether any [Lig]ht comes from the Three Vessels.

**¶2** [If one should wish to ascend,] b[u]t should the [mind] ascend [upwards, it shall reach the] light of the rays that [comes from above,] which are received, which shine and go in through these great open gates, through these doors, firmament by firmament.

**¶3** For four [gates open]pen in each world, distributed at the four points of the compass, opening downwards to the vessels. So, due to this, the [light of the] rays shall go in through these openings and it shines on [that] place, and pulls through them the light and the little pi[llar that ex]ists there.

**¶4** From that place [it] shall be poured [out to the] firmaments. It shall also be clarified in these firmaments [until it becomes distinct, and] a part there is cleansed and it is [all] taken up with the living ones. However, its other part [which is not able to be clarified,] shall all [be cast] down.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶2 [none]: Clean correspondential substrate: ascent of mind is narrated as a functional process of reception and transmission through successive degrees (“firmament by firmament”), with no jurisdictional or name
> - ¶3 [none]: Mostly clean correspondential teaching (ordered receptacles/openings at fourfold boundaries), but there is a likely small textual damage or mistransmission at the phrase “the little pillar that exists
> - ¶4 [none]: Clean correspondential substrate: the teaching proceeds by states and outcomes (clarification/purification; separation of what can be taken up vs cast down). No seam, no systematizing addendum, no gov

**Changes:** No changes made. All findings (¶2, ¶3, ¶4) carried a recommendation of none; ¶3’s localized difficulty (“little pillar”) was not recommended for excision or alteration.

**Spiritual narrative:** When the inner faculty is lifted from lower states toward higher discernment, it comes into the inflowing wisdom that is received from the Divine. This wisdom enters by the established communications that stand open, and it proceeds in order through each successive degree of the mind, degree after degree.

In every distinct level of life there is a complete arrangement of ultimate forms for reception and transmission, set in a full compass of boundaries and turned toward uses below. Through these fixed openings the inflowing wisdom enters into prepared recipient states; it brings illumination to the region it touches, and it draws into operation the truths there together with the small measure of confirmed steadiness present—though the specific force intended by that steadiness is uncertain.

From that illuminated region the same inflow is then communicated onward into the succeeding degrees. In those degrees it is further made distinct and purified: one portion is cleansed of what is discordant and is taken up into conjunction with living affections and thoughts that receive it as life; but the remaining portion, which cannot be clarified because it does not agree with good and truth, is rejected and driven down into lower states.

---

## Chapter 114: Concerning the Three Images that are in the righteous Person.

**¶2** Three images occur in the elect person.

**¶3** The first is the spiritual image, which is the new man, which the Light Mind shall form in him; and it goes into him and dwells in him.

**¶4** The second image is the remnant and remainder of the new man, which is the psychic image that is bound in the flesh, as the old man [is being preserved in the flesh] times of this psychic image.

**¶5** The other one is the corporeal image [that is] added to them all.

**¶6** These three images [operate] entirely. When it will [be elevated] through the nourishment of food. If it shall sink [into it] down to the corporeal image. [And] the corporeal, [that] is in it, shall divest itself of it and place it in the [psychic] image. [Then] again it rises up and falls to the [psychic] image; it shall divest itself of the psychical of the enemies, which [inflame and stir up] anger. The lust [also clings and is carried along] with it.

**¶7** Then shall a light virgin come and reveal the spiritual image that is [there], which [is] the new man. That virgin acts as a guide. [She g]oes on before and it is extended to the heights above, and received into this spiritual image. And she [sculpts] it and adorns it with the new man [wi]thin. It is sealed with all the limbs of this light virgin who is present and dwells in the new man.

**¶8** So, this is [h]ow this living limb shall be [puri]fied and live, the one that comes into the body of [the ri]ghteous one from without through the administration of food of various ki[nds], like this. The living soul shall be clea[n]sed entirely every [d]ay and traverse these three [im]a[ges].

**¶9** So, it shall divest itself of the body, which is not its own, in the corporeal. It shall also divest itself of the souls that are not its own, these that are mixed with it in the psychical [especially in] anger and desire and [dull]-ness and foolishness and envy and strife; and these other wicked teachings that are not its own.

**¶10** However, in [the] spiritual image itself it shall live and be joined with [lo]ng-sufferingness, the perfection of faith and love that reigns over them all. It is the virgin of light who robes the new man and who shall be cal[l]ed ‘the hour of life’. S[he] is the f[irst], but s[h]e is also the last.

> - ¶6 [none]: Genuine difficulty due to heavy corruption/ellipsis in a key process paragraph (state-changes + divesting/transfer). The spiritual reading’s only real ‘stumble’ is syntactic, not conceptual; the under
> - ¶7 [none]: Clean continuation: the ‘light virgin’ functions as an operative mechanism (guide/reveal/seal/organize) within the purification process, not as an administrative office-holder. Personified imagery is 
> - ¶8 [none]: Clean but with one minor potential seam-word (‘administration’) that could mislead; here it still means practical mediation via food, integrated into the functional narrative of daily purification. No
> - ¶9 [none]: Clean ethical unpacking of what gets ‘divested’ at each level; it directly implements the mechanics of ¶6 and stays in correspondential/functional language (not owned vs not its own; mixed states).
> - ¶10 [none]: Clean culmination: positive end-state (faith/love/longsuffering) and identification of the ‘virgin of light’ as the robing/confirming agent; ‘first and last’ is an internal rhetorical closure, not a l

**Changes:** No changes made. The critic’s findings recommend ‘none’ for all paragraphs (Findings 1–9), with ¶4 and ¶6 noted as damaged transmission rather than insertions, and no excisions indicated.

**Spiritual narrative:** In a person being renewed there appear three distinct forms in which life is received and shown.

First, there is an inmost form: higher illumination forms within the person a new will and a new understanding, and this new human is introduced into the person and abides as an interior presence.

Second, there is an intermediate form: what remains of that new human as it is held within the natural self. At this middle level the renewed things are still bound within hereditary selfhood and its acquired habits, because this degree still carries what was formed when the natural self ruled.

Third, there is an outermost form added beneath them, in which life is expressed in the lowest degree through outward action and sensory appearance.

These three forms act together as one process. As the person is sustained by what is received from without, the person’s life is alternately raised toward what is inmost and lowered toward what is outermost, according to the way reception is made. When the life is lowered into the lowest form, the outermost level strips off what does not belong to the person’s proper spiritual life and transfers what can be set in order into the intermediate level. Then the life rises and falls again toward the middle, and in this movement it casts off the middle-level impurities that cling from hostile influences—especially the excitations that inflame contentious anger. Disordered craving also clings and is carried along until it too is separated.

After this, a pure guiding perception from higher illumination approaches and discloses the inmost form that is present, the new human. That guiding illumination goes before, elevating what can be elevated into higher states, and what is elevated is received into the inmost form. It gives the new human determinate shape and orders it with what is renewed within, until the renewal is confirmed and fully organized by all the powers of that illumination which is present and makes its dwelling within the new human.

In this way the living power in a person is purified and truly lives, even as it enters the life of one being made righteous from without through the continual reception of mental sustenance in varied ways. The living soul is to be wholly cleansed each day, and to pass through these three forms of life in their order.

Thus it casts off the outermost selfhood that is not truly its own in the lowest degree. It also casts off the foreign life-admixtures that are not its own, which are mingled in the intermediate degree: irritations, cravings, dullness of mind, empty thinking, jealousy, contention, and similar evils, together with false teachings that do not originate from the new life.

But in the inmost form itself it lives, and it is conjoined with patient endurance, with the completeness of faith, and with love that governs all things in right order. This governing illumination is what invests the new human with living truth, and it is called the turning-point at which life begins. It is first in the work of renewal, and it is also last, because it is the beginning within every stage and the final confirmation of the whole.

---

## Chapter 115: The Catechumen asks the Apostle: will Rest come about for Someone who has come out of the Body, if the Saints pray over and make an Alms-offering for him?

**¶5** As to the question for which you ask me, the entreaty occurs and the true prayer occurs! For every elect person, if he beseeches charity in total faith, he is claiming a request from our compassionate Father. So, if he is beseeching over himself alone, he shall be favoured his entreaty; but if again he is beseeching on behalf of someone else, he shall be granted his request.

**¶6** For this entreaty has been since the beginning [with the first ones, when] the gods first made entreaty of their Father. They were granted their entreaty and their request.

**Changes:** 1) ¶18: Excised the administrative fivefold roster sentence (“The Great Spirit stood with the Beloved of the Lights, the Great Builder with the Living Spirit and the First Man. These five stood to prayer;”) per Finding 5 (recommendation: excise) to restore the functional prayer→request sequence.
2) ¶27: Excised the explicit christological-identificatory phrases (“Also, Jesus the Splendour” and the repeated bare identifications “the power that is Jesus the Splendour” / “Jesus the Splendour,”) per Finding 9 (recommendation: excise), leaving surrounding functional clauses intact.

**Spiritual narrative:** When anyone who is being formed for heaven turns in full trust toward the good of love and asks for it, the very asking is already a true movement of the heart; and because the Divine is compassion itself, that turning is received. If the turning is chiefly for one’s own restoration, it is received according to that need; and if it is chiefly for another’s restoration, it is likewise received, because love looks beyond self and therefore more fully accords with heaven.

This way of asking has existed from the earliest ordering of spiritual life, when the first recipients of heavenly intelligence turned themselves toward the Divine Source and sought aid; and what they sought was granted.

The manner of that ancient asking can be taught: how the earliest states of ordered life sought from the Divine, and how reception is formed when the aim is the right ordering of all things.

The primary wisdom that proceeds from Divine love turned itself wholly to the First Divine, and from affection ascribed all good and all power to that Source. From this humility and love it sought restoring influx and a determinate provision of help, and it received a great communication of strength together with many operative faculties that attend wisdom conjoined to love.

That wisdom sought help for the primal good in a person, because that good had gone forth into ultimates and immersed itself there, becoming remote from its own higher life. It had relinquished itself into distress and entered combat in the lowest degree where destruction, grief, and weakening prevail—within states of falsity and evil, within the deepest fears, amid the compulsions and cravings of hellish persuasions.

For the case was as an inmost governing good beset on every side by contraries. Therefore wisdom, in its turning to the First Divine, sought that a protecting and rescuing power might be sent—an aid for the Divine good held in distress.

This intercession was received into the Divine presence; and the Divine provided what was sought: a great rescuing influx, the living operation of Divine truth that gives relief. Then wisdom and that living operation advanced in power to the outmost limits of the spiritual domain, and from there raised the embattled good out of the struggle in which it had been held.

That delivered good returned as a gift to wisdom; yet it had been made remote, because opposing forces had pressed it into severe distress and weakened it in states devoid of good.

But the living operation of Divine truth took hold of it, liberated it, restored it to order, and gave it tranquility in a state of peace and protection. This relief was according to the united intention of the three—of Divine love, of wisdom from love, and of living truth in act—so that what is received might be welcomed and remain conjoined. (Here the source text’s ending becomes fragmentary and unclear, but the sense of stable reception is preserved.)

Thus the beloved power was brought near again: the primal good that had been far off and fixed in distress. The rescuing operation entered the state of devastation and weakness and by redeeming action released it from affliction, established it in rest and joy, and made its peace enduring.

This was the first ancient turning by which wisdom sought aid, and the aid was granted.

After this, the ordered arrangement of the degrees of life was established and completed: higher and lower structures of reception were set in place; the recurring orderly motions of life were arranged; conveyances of living intelligence in elevated states were provided; the bonds that hold order together were fixed; the external planes of life with their boundaries and defenses were formed; and containing forms for uses were prepared. Thus the whole was made coherent and set into right order by heavenly goods and truths.

Then primary wisdom stood together with beloved illumination, with the forming power that orders, with the living operation of Divine truth, and with the restored primal good. These sufficient powers entered a state of united turning; they acknowledged the First Divine and sought a further provision: that a leading and guiding power might be given for all the uses they had formed, and that this power might come to cleanse the living soul still held in distress.

Their turning was received, and what they sought was granted: a new saving proceeding, a mediating manifestation of Divine operation. It came in the might of Divine greatness and surveyed the whole living soul—how it was bound by compulsions and held fast. It also perceived the ordered works established from higher states, and again perceived the living soul still chained in grievous torment, oppressed by foul delight from the deepest falsity conjoined to it.

Therefore this mediating saving operation, from love, made its manner of deliverance plain even before the deepest hellish state, in order to free the living soul. It redeemed it from deadened cravings and corporeal lusts in which it suffered, brought relief, and restored it into a state of rest and joy.

This saving mediation also fulfilled the united intention of the three, according to the request of the companion powers; thus what had been asked was given in a form suited to effect deliverance.

The third work was the removal and elevation of the living soul that had been freed: it was released from bondage, relieved of affliction, and set into a governing order, becoming primary with respect to the whole formed life.

Thus the second ancient turning has been set forth: the mediating saving operation was granted.

A third turning is introduced, but at this point the record breaks into corrupted fragments and does not yield a continuous spiritual account. Still, the remnants suggest a further manifestation of Divine truth and a concealment from those not prepared; a standing firm of what is Divine in higher states; an unveiling of light; a renewal of the primal good; contempt or mockery from opposites; an investing with outward truths; a liberation of those held; an uprising of confession and praise; and a return to united asking for release through mediating salvation.

What follows indicates conjunction of the living and the redeemed in enduring peace; a first saving separation from contraries; and the giving of hope for endurance.

Also, a more interior Divine manifestation perfected the saving intention so that the will could be ordered according to what is heavenly, and a further power of illumination and rescue was granted. The needs of lower states were answered in order and stability was established. This saving presence humbled itself so it could reach those held in opposition, and its quality was displayed even to adversaries; thus hope was received, and the light-bearing power became manifest as the granted aid for those of heaven. (Here too the source is highly broken, and the sequence can only be held in broad outline.)

And as this turning took an inward form, so too the mind is to receive within itself a stable form of order. For everyone who knows and perceives within themselves the things that flow in from a higher source—how they enter, are received, and are arranged—has within an exemplar through which stability is given.

The first effect of this saving manifestation is the redemption of the living soul bound throughout the whole ordered system of life: it is freed, cleansed, purified, and delivered through that Divine operation.

For the saving power releases the whole person who is held fast in the external self, because it proceeds from the First Divine and carries Divine pity as living efficacy. Through it redemption is made for the soul and the way of deliverance is opened; therefore there is again asking that relief be granted amid distress, and that what is sought be given for the soul that is coming out from bondage, so that it may be set into peace and brought into enduring life.

And the living soul that is freed through the aid of another soul is likewise restored in the name and quality of that person: it is purified, returned to its first essence, and becomes a fellow-helper. From that new state it also seeks aid for the one released from the external self; it desires love and the removal of evils through heavenly truths.

And as relief is given to the living soul through this cooperation, so the cooperating soul also finds relief and is delivered from the external bonds that held it. It is elevated into the state of light; and because life was restored through it, salvation is effected for it as well, and it comes into the enduring state of life and rest.

---

## Chapter 116: Concerning why if a [Nail] is cut the Person shall not be sick; but if a Limb is cut he shall immediately be pained.

**¶5** Happen you know th[at the] body is like the earth, wh[ile these] limbs als[o also] of it are like the trees. There are varieties [of] of tree you shall find growing on the earth. They ar[is]e and grow again and stand firm. They have not [their root in the earth] Again, there are others that if they are cut shall not [grow again] and they grow, as they are li[ke the reeds] which shall be cut and grow [again] and [others] they too that occur [upon the earth] they shall be cut and not grow, resembling [like the dry trees that neither] grow nor shall they [put forth shoot] it [sh]all please him on [what is defiled] and grows [strong in it] in impurity [and draws nourishment] from him. This flesh that is corrupt [and its corruption passes over] in the liv[in]g flesh and they [become weak and] are somewhat sick.

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶5 (opening image: body/earth, limbs/trees) [none]: Clean correspondential setup. The teaching proceeds by FUNCTION and organic analogy: the body is a ground-plane; limbs/faculties are growths that can be cut and may or may not regenerate depending on 
> - ¶5 (middle catalog of plant-types; broken/repetitive clauses) [none]: Genuine difficulty from damage and duplication, not an intrusive administrative overlay. The narrative ‘graded set’ is intact in intention (several kinds of cut-and-return vs cut-and-not-return), but 
> - ¶5 (final turn to impurity/corruption spreading) [none]: Clean continuation and moral application in the same correspondential voice. The passage pivots from the plant-regeneration analogy to the mechanism of corruption as a ‘nourishment’ taken from impurit

**Changes:** No changes made. All findings (1–3) carried a 'none' recommendation, with no excisions or annotations required.

**Spiritual narrative:** You know that the outward level of a person is the ultimate plane where inner things come to fixed form, and that the powers belonging to that outward level are the varied faculties and perceptions that rise within it. Among these faculties there are many kinds. Some arise from interior life, are restored again after loss, and are made stable in use, because their vitality is not merely from the outer plane but from what is living within. Others appear and function for a time, yet when they are deprived of what sustains them they do not return, because they have no inward root in living truth. There are also intermediate states that seem to be removed and then return, because some remnant of inward life still remains; and there are other states that, once destroyed, cannot be restored, because they never had genuine life in them. When the outward self takes delight in what is defiled, it grows strong in disorder and draws its vitality from impurity. Then the outer life becomes corrupted, and this corruption passes over into what ought to be living in the person, so that the healthier faculties are weakened and the whole state becomes sick.

**¶2** The [sec]ond [order comes to him] if he shall fall [from his place,] one [then they set] one hour [one measure for] others who shall [to stand apart,] and they remain. They shall delay until they [have permission until] he is freed, and they delay to come toward [his boundary.]

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶2 [none]: The paragraph is too fragmentary to support a firm voice diagnosis; the spiritual reading’s uncertainty comes from lost syntax and missing agents rather than from an insertion or seam-expansion. What 
> - ¶2 [none]: Within the damage, the surviving phrasing leans toward an administrative/procedural register (“order,” “permission,” boundary/limit language, measured ‘hour/measure’), but it is not a stable administr

**Changes:** No changes made. Finding 1 (¶2) and Finding 2 (¶2) both recommend none; the fragmentary state is attributed to damage rather than insertions, so no excision was performed.

**Spiritual narrative:** When a secondary governing power in the inner order loses its appointed stability, a limited interval is appointed so that what is sufficient for restoring balance may be held in reserve. During that measured state, the assisting powers do not press forward into action, but remain set apart in readiness. They are kept in restraint until permission is given and the bound condition is released. Even after release, they continue for a time in delay, advancing toward the outer limit of operation only as far as restored freedom allows.

**Spiritual narrative:** A complete ordering was laid upon the whole domain of spiritual states, that everything in them be brought into full readiness through an entire process, until a sufficient form of enlightened reception was established for conjunction and use. Then attention turned to a particular recipient. When the appointed fullness of illumination and insight was opened in him, the entire range of his knowledges and all their ordered connections were made manifest to his understanding. He was then to turn wholly toward that illumination, and to live within it as his governing life. This life was not to be claimed as arising from his selfhood, but to be held as a higher gift distinct from the three subordinate degrees within him, which stand beneath and must receive in their own order what flows down from above.

**Changes:** No changes made. All findings (1–6) recommended “none,” with no excisions or annotations required; the noted difficulties are attributed to manuscript damage/corruption rather than intrusive material.

**Spiritual narrative:** Attend to what is now given: the rectitude that belongs to Divine wisdom is more interior and more powerful than any outward utterance that can be formed through lower instruments.

There is a Divine proceeding that the enlightened communities recognized as fit for conjunction with what is highest. They confirmed it within the ordered heavens of wisdom, and the whole abundance of enlightened truths distinguished it from everything mixed. Thus it went forth into resistance against the opposing will. This quality and authority was acknowledged from the first, from the unanimous harmony of all living societies of enlightenment—so many that no finite comprehension can measure them.

They named this proceeding righteous because it was the first purity of truth from good, without any stain of evil, above all heavenly societies that belong to wisdom and above every interior deliberation that would bend it away. For that reason it was recognized inwardly as self-evident to the enlightened.

They also named it the only proceeding because it was established as a single origin and a single essence, and was sent forth for the work of being poured into conflict against the opposing will.

Yet likenesses of this singular proceeding also come forth in lower degrees: as truths from good are generated within a heavenly lineage and are made manifest in the outer person, so too there are proceedings formed even within external riches and knowledges, and within the whole range of the created degree—singular in their own order when they genuinely arise from one ruling love and its wisdom.

Thus the first engaged good itself stood unwavering within the midst of the total domain of evil and falsity. It did not turn love toward that domain, nor did it desire it; rather, within it remained thought and love for the whole community that belongs to it. Whatever the opposing will devised against it, it pressed forward through violent distortions and destructive works. Therefore this engaged good labors and contends so that the opposing will may be separated from those it has invaded, expelled from their interiors, and removed from their life; and it cleanses what had been defiled, releases what had been held, and restores them to the integrity they had at their beginning.

May those who are being cleansed come into living recollection of their own community and its good, because that engaged good remains present with them in every state—whether in obscurity or in clarity—without ceasing.

---

## Chapter 120: Concerning the Two Essences

**¶1** [There are] two essences that are present at the beginning [in the first beginning,] the light and the darkness, that which is good and that which is evi[l], life and] death.

**¶5** For if God has hi[mself] created them, the one who does them [h]as [no] sin therein! If they did not come [about] from him, nor did he command them to be d[on]e, [y]ou are the one who will speak a lie against God, saying [that] all [these e]vil things come about from him [by his command.]

> **Textual criticism:**
> - ¶1 [none]: Clean correspondential (substrate) opening: a compact dual-principle teaching stated by functional oppositions (good/evil; life/death) without administrative apparatus. No narrative seam or alien regi
> - ¶5 [none]: Clean argumentative continuation in the same correspondential/moral-theological mode. It extends the duality into agency and moral responsibility; no intrusion of administrative voice, no list of offi

**Changes:** No changes made. Per Finding 1 (¶1) and Finding 2 (¶5), the critic recommended none (no excisions or annotations), judging both paragraphs coherent in the same correspondential/argumentative voice despite lacunae.

**Spiritual narrative:** At the first arising of any created mind, two opposing principles are present together: openness to wisdom and resistance to wisdom; the turning of the will toward genuine good and its turning toward what destroys good; the reception of spiritual vitality and the choice of spiritual ruin.

If the Divine were the direct producer of both the good impulse and the evil impulse as such, then a person acting from either would carry no guilt, because the act would be traced back to the First Source and not to one’s own appropriated intention. But if evil does not proceed from the Divine and is not willed by the Divine for performance, then to ascribe evils to the Divine is to speak falsity about the Divine, making the very Love and Wisdom that give all good appear to be the cause and commander of what abolishes good and truth in a person.

---

## Chapter 121: Concerning the Sect of the Basket

**¶3** So, [the uni]verse in which you stand is like a tree; while you are like the fruit that hangs from the tree. However, if up till now you are entangled in the universe; how then do you call yourself the son of the basket, before you are plucked from the universe like a fruit? For as long as it hangs from the tree, it shall not be called ‘alms’. And you too, as long as you are entangled in [the] universe you [shall not be] well called the son of the basket.
